- **Scope:** `learning.actions`: `Action(index, buttons)` with `label`; `ActionSpace.from_buttons(buttons, *, combos=True, exclude=("start", "select"))` giving "do nothing", every single button, and every direction + one non-direction button combo (opposite directions never together); `ActionSpace(actions)` with `__len__`, `__getitem__`, `index_of(buttons)`, `buttons_for(index)`, `sample(rng)`, `to_dict`/`from_dict` (so a saved brain keeps its action order), and `matches(buttons)` to map a human's input to the nearest action for demonstrations later. Pause/menu buttons are left out of the learner's menu by default so it can't pause the game on itself. **Out of scope:** the agent (T-503).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The toy racer's seven buttons give: nothing, up, down, left, right, a, b, and the eight direction+button combos (start excluded) in a stable order.
  2. Duplicate or empty button lists, unknown names and bad indices are rejected; `exclude` and `combos=False` work; a space with every button excluded still has "do nothing".
  3. Round-trip through JSON keeps the order; `index_of` / `buttons_for` are inverses; `sample` only returns valid indices; `matches` picks the exact action or the largest subset.
- **Test plan:** toy racer menu; options; validation; JSON and lookups; sampling (hypothesis); matching.
- **Results:** 5 / 5 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (697 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** Combos are direction + one other button only (15 actions on the toy racer); a tabular learner can't afford more, and it covers steer-and-accelerate and run-and-jump.

### T-503 – Tabular Q-learning agent
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 5
- **Objective:** The beginner learner of DESIGN.md §6.1: reward-based, simple state/action learning, tabular Q-learning, with its progress saveable (Phase 5 "save and reload learning progress").
- **Scope:** `learning.qlearning`: `QTable` (state key → one value per action, `best(state)`, `value(state, action)`, `known_states`), `QLearningAgent(space, alpha=0.1, gamma=0.95, epsilon=1.0, epsilon_min=0.05, epsilon_decay=0.995, seed=None)` with `choose(state) -> int` (epsilon-greedy, ties broken at random), `greedy(state)`, `learn(state, action, reward, next_state, done)` (standard Q update, no bootstrap when done), `end_episode()` (decays epsilon), `updates`, `episodes`, `stats()` (states known, updates, exploring share in plain words), `to_dict` / `from_dict` (schema 1, keeps the action space, settings, epsilon and table), `reset()`; settings validated. **Out of scope:** running it against a game (T-504), saving to disk (T-506).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Settings outside their ranges rejected; unknown states start at zero; `best` on an unknown state is action 0 unless a random tie-break is requested.
  2. The Q update matches the textbook formula (including the `done` case); learning is deterministic for a given seed.
  3. Epsilon decays per episode down to its floor; with epsilon 0 the agent is greedy; with epsilon 1 every action gets picked over many draws.
  4. On a tiny two-state chain where only one action leads to reward, the agent learns to prefer it from any state within a few hundred updates.
  5. JSON round-trip reproduces choices and values exactly; a mismatched action space is refused; newer schema refused.
- **Test plan:** validation; update formula; exploration; two-state chain convergence; round-trip and refusals; stats text.
- **Results:** 5 / 5 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (702 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** The random generator's state is saved with the brain so a reloaded agent makes exactly the choices it would have made; `stats()` speaks in "situations" and "goes" rather than states and episodes.

### T-504 – Episode runner
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 5
- **Objective:** Close the loop (DESIGN.md Phase 5 "train through repeated gameplay, reward integration"): the agent looks at the game, presses buttons, the frames advance, the understanding engine raises events, the reward and goal services turn them into points, and the agent learns from them, one step at a time, episode after episode.
- **Scope:** `training.episode`: `EpisodeSignals(bus)` collecting `RewardEarned`, `ObjectiveCompleted` and `ObjectiveSetback` amounts and counting reset events since the last `take()`; `EpisodeRunner(connection, observation, bus, agent, encoder, *, frames_per_step=4, max_steps=600, harness=None)` with `begin()` (optional checkpoint restore, DESIGN.md §9 "checkpoint training"; refreshes the encoder; first state), `step()` (choose, press as the AI, advance, collect reward, encode next state, learn; returns False when the episode is over: a reset in the game, `max_steps`, or the link dropped), `finish()` (release the buttons, count the episode) and `run_episode()` doing all three; `EpisodeResult(steps, frames, reward, ended_by, states_seen)`. Inputs are recorded with source "ai". When nothing has changed for `unstick_after` steps (a game-over screen or a menu, which the learner's menu can't leave because Start is kept out of it), the runner presses the unstick buttons (Start) once without learning from it. **Out of scope:** modes, pause/stop and history (T-505), saving the brain (T-506).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. An episode on the toy racer with a taught knowledge runs the requested steps, presses only actions from the space (recorded as "ai"), advances `frames_per_step` frames per step, learns once per step, and ends by `max_steps`.
  2. A game-over screen is left by pressing Start after `unstick_after` unchanged steps (not learned from, switchable off) and the restart ends the episode with `ended_by == "reset"`; a dropped link ends it with "disconnected" without raising; the buttons are released at the end.
  3. With save states, `begin()` restores the checkpoint so every episode starts from the same place; without them it carries on from wherever the game is.
  4. Rewards collected equal what the services announced (behaviour and objective rewards added together); nothing is collected while no episode is running.
  5. Bad settings rejected; `step()` before `begin()` refused.
- **Test plan:** signals; full episode counts; reset ending; disconnect ending; checkpoint vs no checkpoint; settings and misuse; a short training run on the toy racer that learns some situations.
- **Results:** 7 / 7 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (709 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** The first test on a game-over screen showed the learner can never leave it (Start is kept out of its menu), so the runner now presses Start after a run of unchanged steps. Episode ends come from the engine's own reset event, so the runner never has to know what "game over" looks like in a given game.

### T-505 – Training service
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 5
- **Objective:** Run training as a service the UI and headless scripts can drive (DESIGN.md §8 training modes, §9 live and background training): the AI plays on its own (independent), plays while the child steers rewards and goals (guided), or watches the child play and learns from the demonstration (manual); the child can start, pause, resume and stop; every finished go is kept in a history.
- **Scope:** `training.service`: `Mode` (INDEPENDENT, GUIDED, MANUAL; `label`, `description`, `ai_plays`, `allows_guidance`), `Speed` (LIVE, FAST), `Status` (IDLE, RUNNING, PAUSED), `EpisodeRecord(number, mode, result, frame)`, events `TrainingStateChanged(status, mode, reason)` and `EpisodeFinished(record)`; `TrainingService(connection, observation, discovery, bus, *, frames_per_step=4, max_steps=600, history=200, checkpoint=True, unstick_after=25, seed=None)` with `enabled`, `status`, `mode`, `speed`, `ai_in_control`, `agent`, `space`, `encoder`, `episode` (the running `EpisodeRunner`), `history`, `episodes`, `total_steps`, `last`, `summary()`; controls `set_mode`, `set_speed`, `start(mode=None)`, `pause()`, `resume()`, `stop()`, `step()` (one act-advance-learn step, beginning a new episode when needed; the GUI's timer calls this), `run_for(seconds)` (as many steps as fit, for fast training) and `train(episodes)` (headless). On connect a fresh agent is built from the pad's buttons (`ActionSpace.from_buttons`) and an encoder from the game's knowledge; on disconnect a running go is closed as "disconnected" and training stops. In AI modes, with save states, `start()` takes a checkpoint so every go restarts from where the child left the game (DESIGN.md §9 checkpoint training). In manual mode the service listens to `MemoryObserved` from the play clock and, every `frames_per_step` frames, learns from the buttons the child was holding (matched to the nearest action), with the same rewards. `EpisodeRunner` gains `begin(restore=)` and `observe_step(buttons)` for the demonstration path. **Out of scope:** saving the brain (T-506), the panel and the clock/pad hand-over (T-507), emulator speed control (the GUI steps faster in FAST; adapters' `speed_control` is left for a later task).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Idle and refusing to start until a connected emulator offers memory, input and frame stepping; bad settings and misuse (`pause` when idle, `resume` when running, `train` in manual mode) are refused with clear errors.
  2. Independent and guided: `start` → RUNNING with the AI in control; `step()` acts and learns; a finished go is recorded (`EpisodeFinished`, `history`), the next begins on the next step; `pause` stops stepping and `resume` continues the same go; `stop` closes the go as "stopped", releases the buttons and goes IDLE.
  3. With save states every go begins from the checkpoint taken at `start`; `train(3)` records three goes; `run_for` honours its budget.
  4. Manual: the AI learns one update per `frames_per_step` frames the child plays, from the action matching the held buttons, with the rewards announced meanwhile; nothing is learned while paused; the game's reset closes the go and a new one begins; the AI is not in control.
  5. Switching mode while running closes the current go ("mode change") and carries on in the new mode; switching speed is remembered.
  6. Link loss mid-go records the go as "disconnected", goes IDLE without raising, and a reconnect starts with a fresh agent and an empty history.
  7. `summary()` describes the situation in a child's words; `TrainingStateChanged` is announced for every status or mode change.
- **Test plan:** idle and refusals; independent run with history and stop; pause and resume; checkpoint goes and `train`; manual demonstration learning; mode switch mid-go; disconnect and reconnect; `run_for` and summaries; state-change announcements.
- **Results:** 11 / 11 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (720 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** Two things surfaced in testing. Putting the game back to the checkpoint looks like a restart to the understanding engine, so the runner now starts listening for rewards only after the first observation of a go (otherwise every checkpoint go ended at step one). And link loss closes the go from inside the disconnect handler, so `step()` checks the go is still open before closing it again. In manual mode the service listens to the play clock's observations rather than driving frames itself, which keeps the child's inputs untouched.

### T-506 – Learning persistence
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 5
- **Objective:** What the AI has learned survives closing the app (DESIGN.md Phase 5 "training history", §9 "results can be reviewed afterwards"): the brain for each game is saved to disk and picked up again the next time that game is connected, together with the history of its goes.
- **Scope:** `training.brain`: `Brain(game_id, agent, goes, episodes, total_steps)` with `to_dict` / `from_dict(data, space=)` (schema 1; refuses a newer schema; `EpisodeResult` and `EpisodeRecord` gain `to_dict` / `from_dict`). `storage.brains`: `BrainStore(folder)` mirroring the other stores (`path_for`, `games`, `exists`, `save(brain)`, `load(game_id)`, `delete`) plus `export_brain` / `load_brain`, keeping the file plain JSON so the storage layer stays below training. `TrainingService(..., store=None)` gains `store`, `game_id`, `saves`, `fresh_start` (why a saved brain wasn't used, if it wasn't), `brain()`, `save()` and `forget()` (start the AI from scratch and delete its file; refused while a go is running); on connect a saved brain for the game is loaded when its controls match the connected game's, otherwise (or on a damaged file) a fresh one starts and `fresh_start` says why; the brain is saved after every finished go and on disconnect. `AppContext` gains `brain_store` under `data_dir / "brains"`. **Out of scope:** the panel (T-507), exporting / importing brains by hand from the UI (later phase).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A brain round-trips through the store with its Q-table, settings, exploration rate, random state, counts and goes; a damaged or newer file is refused with a clear error; the store lists games and deletes.
  2. Reconnecting the same game restores the agent (same table, counts, epsilon) and the history; `summary()` says how much it already knows.
  3. A brain saved for a game with different controls, or a damaged file, gives a fresh brain and `fresh_start` says why; nothing raises.
  4. The brain is saved after every go and on disconnect; `save()` works by hand; nothing is saved without a store or while disconnected.
  5. `forget()` wipes the brain and its file while idle and is refused mid-go.
  6. The app context wires the store.
- **Test plan:** brain round-trip and refusals; store listing and deletion; reconnect restores; mismatched controls and damaged file; autosave on go end and disconnect; forget; app context.
- **Results:** 8 / 8 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (728 in the full suite; two T-505 tests updated for the restored history). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** `Mode`, `Speed`, `Status` and `EpisodeRecord` moved to `training.records` so the brain can import them without pulling in the services. The brain file keeps the agent's random state, so a reloaded AI makes exactly the choice it would have made. Go numbering carries on across sessions; the history kept in memory is bounded, so `episodes` is stored separately from the goes.

### T-507 – Learning panel
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 5
- **Objective:** Give the child the Learning panel of DESIGN.md §10: pick a mode (on its own / with your help / you play), Start, Pause, Stop, watch it play or practise fast, see what the AI is doing and how its recent goes went; and make the game's own controls yield to the AI while it plays (§9 live training).
- **Scope:** `ui.learning_panel`: `LearningPanel(context)` in the main window's "learning" slot with `mode_combo`, `speed_combo`, `start_button`, `pause_button` (Pause / Resume), `stop_button`, `forget_button` ("Start from scratch"), `learning_status` (the service's summary, what the AI is pressing, what it knows, and why a saved brain wasn't used), `goes_tree` (Go | How | Steps | Points | Ended by; newest first, up to 30) and `learning_note`; a `QTimer` ticker that, while the AI plays, takes one step per game tick in LIVE (`frames_per_step` frames' worth of time) and a 20 ms slice of `run_for` per tick in FAST, and stays off in manual mode (the play clock drives) and while paused; `tick()` for tests. `EpisodeRunner` gains `last_buttons`. `ControllerPad` gains `set_locked(locked)` (held buttons released, pad greyed, "The AI has the controls" message; presses refused). `GamePanel` listens to `TrainingStateChanged`: while `training.ai_in_control` the play clock is paused, the pad locked and the Play button disabled; afterwards the pad unlocks and the clock resumes if the child had it playing. `MainWindow.learning_panel`. **Out of scope:** learning graphs (Phase 6 T-6xx), locking reward edits in independent mode (left to the child's judgement for now; noted for the Phase 6 review).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Idle until connected: controls disabled and the status says to connect; on connect the mode and speed pickers reflect the service and Start is enabled.
  2. Start runs the AI: the ticker is active, each tick steps the service, Pause reads "Resume" when paused and ticks do nothing, Stop closes the go, lists it, and goes idle; FAST takes several steps per tick.
  3. While the AI plays, the game panel's play clock is paused, the pad is locked and refuses presses, and the Play button is disabled; after Stop the pad works again and the clock resumes if it had been playing.
  4. In manual mode the clock keeps running, the pad stays live, the ticker stays off, and the child's play teaches the AI.
  5. "Start from scratch" is refused (with a note) while running and wipes the brain when idle; link loss mid-go leaves the panel idle and disabled with a note.
  6. Every error from the service shows in the note rather than raising; the panel unsubscribes when destroyed.
- **Test plan:** idle and connect; start, tick, pause, resume, stop and the goes list; fast ticks; hand-over with the game panel in a main window; manual mode; forget and disconnect; notes and teardown.
- **Results:** 7 / 7 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (735 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** Phase 5 is complete: the AI can be started from the window, plays the toy racer on its own or learns from the child, and its brain is kept between sessions. The hand-over lives in the game panel (it listens for training state changes) so the Learning panel never reaches into another panel. A test showed that a disconnect note was wiped by the disconnect event itself, so the panel now clears notes only on connect.


### Phase 6 breakdown
Live and background training and the guided and manual modes were delivered in Phase 5 (T-505, T-507), so Phase 6 covers the rest of DESIGN.md §16 "Training Interface" together with the §10 panel contents not yet shown (AI decisions, training attempts, success rate, trends, behaviour changes, suggested experiments), the §12 explanations, and the §17 demonstration on the Fake Emulator. Tasks T-601 to T-607 as in the summary table; each gets its own section when started.

### T-601 – Learning curve
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 6
- **Objective:** Turn the history of goes into something a child can read and a graph can draw (DESIGN.md §10 Learning "learning progress", Progress "training attempts, success rate, performance trends", §16 Phase 6 "learning graphs, training history").
- **Scope:** `training.curve`: `CurvePoint(number, reward, steps, average)` and `LearningCurve(goes, window=10)` with `points` (one per go, `average` the rolling mean over the last `window` goes), `rewards`, `best` (highest-scoring go), `longest`, `latest_average`, `earlier_average` (the window before the latest), `trend` ("improving", "slipping", "steady" or "too early" before `2 * window` goes; a change counts when it exceeds a tenth of the rewards' spread), `attempts`, `successes` and `success_rate` (goes in which at least one objective was completed), `describe()` in a child's words; `EpisodeSignals` counts objective completions and `EpisodeResult` gains `objectives` (default 0 for files saved before); `TrainingService.curve(window=)`. **Out of scope:** drawing (T-602), showing it in the panels (T-602/T-603).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Points, rolling averages, best and longest goes match a hand-computed example; the empty curve is safe everywhere.
  2. Trend is "too early" with fewer than `2 * window` goes, then improving / slipping / steady as the latest window compares with the one before, ignoring tiny changes.
  3. Success rate counts goes with a completed objective; runner results carry the count; old saved results load with 0.
  4. `describe()` reads naturally in every case; `window` must be at least 1.
- **Test plan:** hand-computed curve; empty and single-go curves; trend cases; success rate from runner results and from loaded data; description texts; service integration.
- **Results:** 7 / 7 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (742 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** Phase 6 was broken into T-601..T-607 before starting. Reviewing the service for this task found that a link dropping while the start checkpoint was being taken left training "running" with no game; `start()` now refuses with a clear message. The link-loss panel test was made deterministic (LIVE ticks rather than timed FAST slices).

### T-602 – Learning graph widget
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 6
- **Objective:** Show the learning curve as a picture in the Learning panel (DESIGN.md §10 "learning progress", §16 "learning graphs"): one dot per go for the points it scored, a line for the rolling average, the best go marked, and a caption in plain words.
- **Scope:** `ui.learning_graph`: `plot_points(curve, width, height, margin)` (pure geometry: x spaced evenly by go, y scaled so the zero line is drawn when rewards straddle it; returns `PlotLayout(dots, line, zero_y, best)` in widget pixels) and `LearningGraph(QWidget)` with `set_curve(curve)`, `curve`, `caption` (the curve's `describe()`, or an invitation when there are no goes), `layout()` (the geometry for the current size), `paintEvent` drawing background, zero line, dots (green for gains, orange for losses, the best ringed), average line and a one-line legend; `minimumHeight` 120. `LearningPanel` gains `graph` (between the status and the goes list), refreshed with the goes list. **Out of scope:** zooming, several curves at once (experiment comparison, Phase 8), exporting the picture.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Geometry is right by hand: evenly spaced goes, the highest reward at the top margin, the lowest at the bottom, the zero line between them when rewards straddle zero and absent otherwise; a single go sits centred; the empty curve gives an empty layout.
  2. The widget draws something different once a curve is set (a rendered image is no longer blank) and its caption follows the curve's description; the empty caption invites the child to press Start.
  3. The Learning panel shows the graph and updates it as goes finish and on connect / disconnect.
- **Test plan:** geometry by hand (several, one, none, all-negative, all-positive); rendering changes; caption texts; panel integration.
- **Results:** 4 / 4 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (746 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** Drawn with QPainter rather than a plotting library, so the Windows build gains no dependency; the geometry is a pure function and the rendering test checks pixel colours at the computed dot positions.

### T-603 – Progress panel training stats
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 6
- **Objective:** Fill in the Progress panel's missing lines from DESIGN.md §10: training attempts, success rate and performance trend, next to the goal and its steps.
- **Scope:** `ui.goals_panel`: `training_stats_text(curve)` (pure: "No training tries yet." / "Tries: N. Reached a goal in S of them (P%). Trend: getting better / slipping / holding steady / too early to say.") and a `training_stats` label under the goal summary, refreshed on `EpisodeFinished`, `TrainingStateChanged` and connect / disconnect; `stats_text` property. **Out of scope:** per-step success rates (which steps are reached most often; a later refinement), graphs here (the Learning panel has the graph).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The helper formats every case (none, some successes, each trend) in plain words.
  2. The label is empty while disconnected, says "No training tries yet." on connect, and counts goes as they finish, including goes loaded back from the saved brain.
- **Test plan:** helper cases; panel after training; disconnect / reconnect.
- **Results:** 2 / 2 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (748 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** A small task by design: the numbers come from T-601's curve, so the panel only formats them. Per-step success rates are noted as a later refinement.

### T-604 – AI activity
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 6
- **Objective:** Show what the AI is doing right now (DESIGN.md §10 "AI Activity": current action, previous actions, current objective, AI decisions) at the top of the "What the AI is doing" panel, in a child's words.
- **Scope:** `learning.qlearning`: `Decision(action, explored, value)` recorded by `choose()` as `last_decision` (cleared by `reset()` / `end_episode()` is left alone so the last move stays visible). `ui.activity_strip`: `ActivityStrip(context)` with three lines, `now_text` ("Resting." / "Now: pressing up + a, trying something new." / "Now: pressing up, its best known move (worth +3.2)." / "Watching you press up." in manual mode), `before_text` ("Before: up, up + a, nothing, …", the last 8 moves, newest first; collected from `InputRecorded` events of the player in charge) and `goal_text` ("Working on: Get further in Checkpoint." / "No goal step to work on."); refreshed on `InputRecorded`, `TrainingStateChanged`, `EpisodeFinished`, `GoalChanged`, `ObjectiveCompleted` and connect / disconnect. `DiscoveryPanel.activity` hosts it above the stage line. **Out of scope:** why a move is best (Q-values per button; a later "look inside" view), screen overlays.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `choose()` records whether the move was exploration or the best known one and its value; a fresh agent with `epsilon=1` always explores, with `epsilon=0` never does.
  2. Idle text while nothing runs; during an AI go the now-line names the pressed buttons and whether it was exploring or its best move; previous moves list the last 8, newest first; in manual mode the strip reports the child's presses.
  3. The goal line follows the goal service's next step and clears when the goal is complete or absent.
  4. Disconnect resets the strip; the panel hosts it and it unsubscribes when destroyed.
- **Test plan:** agent decisions; strip texts through an AI go, manual play and disconnect; goal line; teardown.
- **Results:** 6 / 6 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (754 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** Listening to `InputRecorded` turned out wrong: a repeated "do nothing" records no input, so the strip missed moves. The service now publishes `TrainingStepped(frame, buttons, decision)` for every step (both modes), and the strip redraws at most once per event-loop turn so fast training doesn't flood the labels. The runner exposes `last_action` (None for an unstick press) so the strip can say "to get the game going again".

### T-605 – Behaviour changes and suggested experiments
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 6
- **Objective:** Fill the last two lines of the Learning panel from DESIGN.md §10 ("AI behaviour changes", "identified improvements", "suggested experiments"): tell the child how the AI's habits have changed over recent goes and what to try next, in plain words.
- **Scope:** `training.insights`: `policy_of(table)` (the best known move per situation), `compare_policies(before, after)` → `BehaviourChange(state_key, before, after)` for situations whose best move changed, `describe_changes(changes, known, goes_ago, knowledge, space)` ("Over the last 10 goes the AI changed its mind in 12 of 40 situations. Where Lane=2, Speed=3 it used to press up; now it presses up + a."), `Experiment(title, why)` and `suggest_experiments(curve, agent, warning, goal, profile)` (rule-based, at most three: keep going while it is still mostly exploring; practise fast when the trend is too early; raise the reward for the step it is working on when nothing has been reached; lower a farmed reward when the exploit detector is warning; add a harder step when goals are reached often; try another personality when it is slipping). `TrainingService` keeps a snapshot of the policy at the end of every go (the last `window + 1`) and offers `behaviour_changes(window=)` and `experiments()`. `LearningPanel` gains `learning_insights` (`insights_text`) under the graph, refreshed when a go finishes. **Out of scope:** comparing two AIs (Phase 8 experiments), explaining a single move's values (a later "look inside" view).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Policies compare by hand; descriptions name situations with the knowledge's variable names and moves with the space's labels; nothing to say before two snapshots exist.
  2. Each suggestion rule fires on its case and not otherwise; at most three are offered; every suggestion has a reason.
  3. After goes the service reports changes against the snapshot `window` goes ago (or the oldest kept); the panel shows the text and updates per go; disconnect clears it.
- **Test plan:** policy comparison and wording; suggestion rules; service snapshots over goes; panel text.
- **Results:** 4 / 4 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (757 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** The suggestions are deliberately simple rules with a reason attached, so a child can see why each is offered; they will be revisited once real games show which ones help. Policy snapshots are taken once per go, so comparing habits costs nothing during play.

### T-606 – First working demonstration on the Fake Emulator
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 6
- **Objective:** Prove DESIGN.md §17 end to end on Toy Racer with nothing taught by hand: connect, observe, list the buttons, examine memory, find the useful state, let the child set rewards, train towards a simple objective, show decisions and progress, change the rewards, and show that the change alters how the AI plays.
- **Scope:** `tests/integration/test_first_demonstration.py`: one scripted run through the ten steps using only the services the panels drive (a 2400-frame scripted "first go" by a child, discovery on its own, `RewardService.set_value / set_enabled`, a hand-written "Score some points" step, 30 goes of independent training per reward profile from the same starting line, the curve, behaviour changes and experiment suggestions, persistence across a reconnect). Fixes the run uncovered: `learning.state` ignores `COUNTER` variables (frame counters made every situation unique); `rewards.goals.merge_suggestions` retires a suggested step whose variable the AI no longer thinks tracks progress even if it was "completed" (`Goal.reword` keeps the suggested flag when only the wording changes); `training.episode.EpisodeRunner(settle_frames=5)` ignores a reset seen within the engine's event window of a checkpoint restore, which used to end one go in ten at step 1. CI then showed that PySide6 6.12.0 on Python 3.11 drops one reference to `True` on every Qt signal emission and aborts the interpreter after a few thousand events (the demonstration sends tens of thousands): `pyproject.toml` keeps PySide6 below 6.12 on Python 3.11 (6.11.3 verified clean; 6.12.0 is fine on 3.12+) and `tests/ui/test_bridge.py` gains a guard that forwards 3000 events and checks the reference counts of `True`, `False` and `None` don't move. **Out of scope:** a real emulator (Phase 7), the GUI driving the same script (the panels are covered by their own tests), tuning the agent for Toy Racer.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Without teaching, discovery classifies lives, score and speed correctly and the frame counter as a counter, and the state encoder leaves counters out.
  2. A reward profile is suggested and can be changed; a child's objective completes during training and is counted in the goes.
  3. Every step publishes a decision (both exploring and best-known moves occur); the curve, behaviour changes, summary and experiment suggestions all have something to say after 30 goes.
  4. With points rewarded and crashes free the AI drives at least twice as fast, scores more and runs out of lives sooner than with crashes punished and points worthless; the first profile earns positive reward and the second negative.
  5. Rewards, the goal and the (forgotten then retrained) brain come back after a reconnect.
- **Test plan:** the demonstration test (marked `slow`, about two seconds); a bridge test that forwards thousands of events and checks no reference leaks; unit tests for the three fixes (counter ignored by the encoder; stale completed suggestion retired, child's step and orphan kept, renamed variable reworded; reset within `settle_frames` of a restore ignored, later resets and resets without a restore honoured, negative value rejected).
- **Results:** 5 / 5 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (761 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 with PySide6 6.12.0 and on Python 3.11 with PySide6 6.11.3). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** Measured on fake seed 5 with agent seed 7, over the last 10 of 30 goes: points-lover mean speed 1.20, score 331, 90 steps, reward +7.6; careful AI speed 0.39, score 247, 150 steps, reward −82. The careful AI still clips obstacles while exploring (an obstacle stops the car and costs a life), so "never crashes" is not claimed; the robust difference is speed and survival. Discovery still calls the checkpoint byte a position and the lap a flag at 2400 frames, so the suggested goal steps are switched off for the run and the child's own step is used; better progress detection is a Phase 7 refinement. Milestone 1 of DESIGN.md §17 is met on the synthetic game.

### T-607 – Educational tips
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 6
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §12: every panel can explain itself and the ideas behind it in simple words, with an advanced explanation for older children and adults, covering all ten listed topics (AI, machine learning, reinforcement learning, rewards and penalties, neural networks, training and experimentation, decision-making, exploration versus exploitation, reward exploitation, hierarchical learning).
- **Scope:** `education.tips`: pure data. `Tip(key, title, simple, advanced, see_also)`, `TIPS` (one per panel key plus one per §12 topic), `tip_for(key)` (unknown key raises `ConfigurationError`), `TOPIC_KEYS`, `PANEL_TIP` mapping panel key to tip key. `ui.tips`: `TipDialog(parent)`, a non-modal window with a topic list, a "Use grown-up words" switch that is remembered while the app runs, the explanation, and "See also" buttons that jump between tips; `show_tip(key)`, `current_key`, `advanced`, `set_advanced(bool)`, `title_text`, `body_text`, `see_also_keys()`. `ui.panels`: each `Panel` header gets a "What's this?" button (`help_button`) emitting `help_requested(key)`; `MainWindow.tip_dialog` opens on it. **Out of scope:** lessons or quizzes, per-widget tooltips beyond the panel level (later refinement), translations.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. There is a tip for every panel and every §12 topic; keys are unique; every tip has a title, a simple text and a longer advanced text; simple texts avoid jargon ("algorithm", "policy", "Q-value", "epsilon", "gradient", "hyperparameter"); every see-also key exists.
  2. `tip_for` raises a clear error for an unknown key.
  3. Clicking a panel's "What's this?" shows that panel's tip; the switch changes the text and stays switched for the next tip; see-also buttons move to the linked tip; the topic list reaches every tip.
  4. The main window owns one dialog shared by all five panels; showing it twice doesn't open two windows.
- **Test plan:** data completeness, jargon and link checks; unknown key; dialog through the panel buttons with level switching and see-also; main window wiring.
- **Results:** 6 / 6 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (766 in the full suite). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Completion report:** The tips live in a new `education` package as plain data, so the same words can later feed tooltips or a printed sheet. Each simple telling is checked against a short jargon list by the tests, so a later edit can't quietly slip "epsilon" into the child's version. Phase 6 is complete: DESIGN.md §10, §12, §16 and §17 are covered on the Fake Emulator.

### Phase 7 breakdown
DESIGN.md §6 and §16 "Advanced Learning": neural networks, DQN, hierarchical learning, optional demonstration learning and more sophisticated game understanding. None of it needs a real emulator, so it is built and proven on the Fake Emulator while ADR-005 / ADR-006 wait. Networks run on NumPy (ADR-009). Tasks T-701 to T-709 as in the summary table; each gets its own section when started.

### T-701 – Learner interface
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 7
- **Objective:** Put every learning algorithm behind one contract so the training service, the saved brain, the insights and the panels work the same whether the AI learns with a table or a network (DESIGN.md §6 "the learning engine must be modular").
- **Scope:** `learning.learner`: a runtime-checkable `Learner` protocol (`algorithm` name, `space`, `epsilon`, `updates`, `episodes`, `known`, `last_decision`, `stats()`, `choose`, `greedy`, `learn`, `end_episode`, `reset`, `policy()` – the best known move per known situation – and `to_dict()`), `Decision` moved here, a registry (`register_learner`, `algorithms()`, `make_learner(name, space, settings, seed)`, `learner_from_dict(data, space)` dispatching on the saved `algorithm`, with `q_table` the default for brains saved before this task). `QLearningAgent` implements it (`algorithm = "q_table"`, `known`, `policy()`, `algorithm` in its JSON). `Brain.agent`, `EpisodeRunner` and `TrainingService` are typed to `Learner`; the service takes `algorithm=` and builds through the registry; `insights.policy_of` takes a learner. **Out of scope:** any new algorithm (T-704/T-705), the UI choice (T-706).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `QLearningAgent` satisfies `Learner` at runtime and `policy()` names the best move for every situation it knows.
  2. `make_learner` builds a learner by name with the given settings and seed; an unknown name raises `ConfigurationError` naming the choices.
  3. `learner_from_dict` rebuilds the right class from saved data, treats data without an `algorithm` as `q_table`, and rejects an unknown algorithm or mismatched controls.
  4. A brain round-trips through the registry and reports its algorithm; a brain claiming an unknown algorithm is refused with a clear message.
  5. Behaviour changes and experiments work on any learner through the protocol (proven with a stub learner that has no Q-table).
  6. `TrainingService(algorithm=...)` validates the name up front; every existing training, panel and demonstration test still passes.
- **Expected changes:** `learning/learner.py` (new), `learning/qlearning.py`, `learning/__init__.py`, `training/brain.py`, `training/episode.py`, `training/insights.py`, `training/service.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_learner.py` (new), `tests/unit/training/test_insights.py`.
- **Test plan:** unit – protocol conformance and `policy()`; registry by name, unknown name, settings and seed passed through; `from_dict` with and without `algorithm`, unknown algorithm, wrong controls; brain round-trip and refusal; insights on a stub learner; service rejects a bad algorithm and saves the algorithm name. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 / 5 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (772 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 with PySide6 6.12.0 and on Python 3.11 with PySide6 6.11.3). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #50.
- **Completion report:** The registry holds learner classes by `algorithm` name; `learner_from_dict` reads the name from the saved data and defaults to `q_table`, so every brain saved so far still loads unchanged. `Decision` moved to `learning.learner` (still importable from `learning`). Insights now ask the learner for its `policy()` instead of walking a Q-table, proven with a stub learner that has no table. The service validates the algorithm name in its constructor and builds new brains (connect, forget) through the registry. ADR-009 records that Phase 7 networks are NumPy behind this interface. Known limitation: only `q_table` is registered until T-705.

### T-702 – Experience replay
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 7
- **Objective:** Give learners a bounded memory of past steps they can learn from again, in random order (DESIGN.md §6.1 "experience replay"), ready for the DQN (T-705) and for remembering the child's play (T-707).
- **Scope:** `learning.replay`: `Transition(state, action, reward, next_state, done, source="ai")` (frozen; reward must be finite, action non-negative) and `ReplayBuffer(capacity, seed=None)` with `append`, `extend`, `__len__`, `__iter__`, `capacity`, `added` (everything ever stored), `latest(n)`, `sample(n)` (distinct transitions, seeded), `clear()`, and a JSON round trip (`to_dict` / `from_dict`) that keeps order, counts and the random state. **Out of scope:** prioritised replay, using the buffer in a learner (T-705), recording the child's play (T-707).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The buffer keeps at most `capacity` transitions, dropping the oldest first, and `added` counts every one ever stored.
  2. `sample(n)` returns `n` distinct transitions from the buffer and is reproducible for a seed; asking for more than it holds, for fewer than one, or from an empty buffer raises `ConfigurationError`.
  3. Invalid construction (capacity below one, a non-finite reward, a negative action) raises `ConfigurationError`; transitions are immutable.
  4. A buffer round-trips through JSON with its order, capacity, `added` and random state, so the next `sample` after a reload matches; saved data holding more than `capacity` keeps the newest; bad data is refused.
  5. For any sequence of appends the buffer holds exactly the last `min(n, capacity)` of them (property test).
- **Expected changes:** `learning/replay.py` (new), `learning/__init__.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_replay.py` (new).
- **Test plan:** unit – append / eviction / `added` / `latest`; sampling size, distinctness, seeding and the three errors; validation and immutability; JSON round trip incl. oversize data and bad data; hypothesis property over random append sequences. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 / 5 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (777 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #51.
- **Completion report:** `Transition` and `ReplayBuffer` as planned; transitions carry a `source` ("ai" or "child") so T-707 can keep the child's play apart from the AI's. The buffer is a `deque(maxlen)` with its own seeded `Random`, so sampling after a reload continues the same sequence. Nothing uses it yet; T-705 wires it into the DQN.

### T-703 – Feature vectors
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 7
- **Objective:** Turn a situation (a `State`) into a fixed-length vector of numbers a neural network can read, so a network learner (T-705) sees the same situations as the Q-table and a saved network keeps seeing them the same way next session.
- **Scope:** `learning.features`: `FeatureMap(width=DEFAULT_WIDTH, bins=DEFAULT_BINS)` that gives each believed variable two slots in first-seen order – "present" (0 / 1) and its bucket scaled to [-1, 1] (`bucket / (bins + 16)`, clipped) – so a variable that is missing from a state reads as absent rather than as "bucket 0"; `vector(state) -> float32 (size,)` with `size = 2 * width`; `batch(states) -> (n, size)`; `ids`, `slot_of(id)`, `dropped` (variables seen beyond `width`, which are ignored and counted); JSON round trip (`to_dict` / `from_dict`) that keeps the slot order. **Out of scope:** the network (T-704), raw pixels or memory as features, learned embeddings.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Variables get slots in the order they are first seen and keep them; the map grows as new variables appear without moving existing slots; `size` is fixed from the start.
  2. A present variable reads as `(1, bucket / (bins + 16))`, an absent one as `(0, 0)`, and negative buckets as negative numbers; every value lies in [-1, 1] for any bucket the encoder can produce (property test).
  3. `batch` returns an `(n, size)` float32 array whose rows equal the single vectors; an empty state is all zeros; two states that differ (within the width) give different vectors.
  4. `width` below one or `bins` outside 2..64 raise `ConfigurationError`; variables past the width are ignored and counted in `dropped`, and the map still works.
  5. A map round-trips through JSON (slot order, width, bins, dropped) so a reloaded map gives identical vectors; bad data is refused with `ConfigurationError`.
- **Expected changes:** `learning/features.py` (new), `learning/__init__.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_features.py` (new).
- **Test plan:** unit – slot assignment and growth; vector values (present / absent / negative) and a hypothesis property over buckets and bins for the [-1, 1] bound; batch shape, dtype, zeros and distinctness; validation and overflow; JSON round trip and bad data. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 6 / 6 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (783 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #52.
- **Completion report:** `FeatureMap` as planned: two slots per variable (present, scaled bucket), slots handed out in first-seen order and kept, `size = 2 * width` fixed from construction so a network's input layer never has to change. The scale is `bins + 16`, the largest bucket a 16-bit value reaches, and values are clipped to [-1, 1] regardless. Variables beyond the width are ignored and counted in `dropped` (a documented limit; the default width of 32 is far above what the engine believes in practice). The map saves with the slot order so a reloaded network reads identical vectors. Nothing uses it yet; T-705 does.

### T-704 – NumPy neural network
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 7
- **Objective:** A small, dependable neural network with no new dependency (ADR-009) that the DQN learner (T-705) can train on feature vectors: dense layers, a loss on chosen outputs, Adam, copies for a target network and a JSON round trip.
- **Scope:** `learning.network`: `Network(sizes, *, seed=None, learning_rate=1e-3)` – dense layers with ReLU between and a linear output, He-initialised from the seed, float64 parameters; `forward(x) -> (n, out)`; `loss(x, targets, mask=None)` – mean Huber loss over the outputs the mask selects (all outputs when none); `gradients(x, targets, mask=None) -> (loss, grads)`; `train_step(x, targets, mask=None) -> loss` applying one Adam update; `copy()` (independent weights, fresh optimiser state) and `load_from(other)` (sync a target network); `parameters` (the live arrays, for the gradient check), `steps`; `to_dict` / `from_dict` keeping weights, the optimiser state and the step count. **Out of scope:** convolutions, GPU, PyTorch, the learner itself (T-705).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `forward` returns `(n, out)` finite values for any `(n, in)` input; the same seed gives the same network, a different seed a different one; bad sizes or learning rate raise `ConfigurationError`, and a wrong input width raises `ConfigurationError`.
  2. `gradients` match central finite differences on every parameter of a random small network to a relative error below 1e-6 (gradient check).
  3. Adam fits a simple target function: the loss falls by at least 100× within 500 steps; a mask makes training ignore the outputs it leaves out (their loss contribution is zero and they barely move).
  4. `copy()` gives a network that forwards identically but trains independently; `load_from` makes two networks forward identically again; mismatched sizes are refused.
  5. A network round-trips through JSON (weights, optimiser state, steps) so that one more `train_step` after a reload gives the same loss and weights as without the reload; bad data is refused.
  6. The Huber loss is never negative and is zero when outputs equal targets (property test).
- **Expected changes:** `learning/network.py` (new), `learning/__init__.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_network.py` (new).
- **Test plan:** unit – shapes, determinism, validation; finite-difference gradient check; fitting and masking; copy / load_from; JSON round trip and bad data; hypothesis property on the loss. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 6 / 6 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (789 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #53.
- **Completion report:** `Network` as planned: He-initialised dense layers, ReLU hidden units, linear outputs, a masked mean Huber loss, hand-written backpropagation checked against central finite differences on every parameter (relative error below 1e-6), Adam with bias correction, `copy()` / `load_from()` for a target network, and a JSON round trip that includes the optimiser moments and step count so training continues exactly after a reload. Parameters are float64 (tiny networks; exactness over speed). Nothing uses it yet; T-705 does.

### T-705 – DQN learner
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 7
- **Objective:** The intermediate learner of DESIGN.md §6.1: a Deep Q-Network that learns from feature vectors with experience replay and a target network, behind the same `Learner` contract as the Q-table, so the training service, the saved brain, the panels and the insights work unchanged.
- **Scope:** `learning.dqn`: `DQN = "dqn"`; `DQNConfig(hidden=(64, 64), batch_size=32, warmup=32, target_sync=250, learning_rate=1e-3, replay_capacity=10_000, width=32)` (validated); `DQNLearner(space, settings=None, *, seed=None, config=None)` registered as `"dqn"` – epsilon-greedy over the online network's Q-values (`Settings` gives gamma and the exploration schedule; `alpha` is unused), `learn` stores the transition in a `ReplayBuffer` and, once `warmup` transitions are held, trains the network on a random batch towards `r + γ · max Q_target(s')` (0 beyond `done`) with the chosen action masked, syncing the target network every `target_sync` training steps; `known` / `policy()` over the distinct situations in memory (bounded by the replay capacity); `stats()` in plain words; `reset()`; `to_dict` / `from_dict` saving the feature map, both networks, the replay memory, the known situations, the random states and the counts so learning continues exactly after a reload. `algorithms()` becomes `("dqn", "q_table")`. **Out of scope:** choosing the algorithm automatically and the UI control (T-706), demonstrations (T-707), Double DQN / prioritised replay.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `DQNLearner` satisfies `Learner`, builds through `make_learner("dqn", ...)`, reports `algorithm == "dqn"`, and refuses a bad action index, a non-finite reward and a bad config.
  2. It learns: on the two-state chain and on a six-step corridor (reward only at the end) the greedy policy goes right from every situation within a bounded number of steps, with the value nearer the reward higher.
  3. Replay and target mechanics: nothing is trained before `warmup` transitions; every `learn` after that trains once on `batch_size` transitions; the target network equals the online one right after each sync and only then.
  4. Exploration decays per episode to the floor; `reset()` empties memory and knowledge and restores exploration; `known` and `policy()` cover exactly the situations seen; `stats()` names them.
  5. A learner round-trips through JSON (`learner_from_dict` dispatches to it): the same Q-values, the same next choices and the same next training step as without the reload; wrong controls, wrong algorithm, a newer schema and damaged data are refused.
  6. The training service runs goes with `algorithm="dqn"` on the Fake Emulator, saves the brain and restores it on reconnect; insights work on the DQN's policy.
- **Expected changes:** `learning/dqn.py` (new), `learning/__init__.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_dqn.py` (new), `tests/unit/learning/test_learner.py` (algorithm list).
- **Test plan:** unit – conformance and validation; learning on the chain and the corridor (seeded); warmup / batch / target-sync bookkeeping; decay, reset, known, policy, stats; JSON round trip and refusals; training service end to end with `algorithm="dqn"`. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 6 / 6 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (795 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #54.
- **Completion report:** `DQNLearner` as planned, registered as `"dqn"`. It reuses `Settings` for gamma and the exploration schedule (`alpha` is unused; the network has its own learning rate in `DQNConfig`), the feature map, the network and the replay buffer from T-702..T-704. The learning test shows it solving the two-state chain in 400 steps and a six-square corridor with a reward only at the end in 1500 steps with a 16-unit hidden layer. The saved brain holds both networks, the replay memory, the known situations and the random states, so the next choice and the next training step after a reload are bit-for-bit the same; a brain saved without the memory or the random states still loads. The service test runs three goes with `algorithm="dqn"` on the Fake Emulator and restores the brain on reconnect. Known limitation: the replay memory makes the saved brain larger (about 200 bytes per transition, 10 000 at most).

### T-706 – Automatic algorithm choice and the "How the AI learns" control
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 7
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §6.1: "select appropriate algorithms automatically by default" while "advanced users can experiment with different approaches". The AI starts with the simple table and moves to the neural network when the game grows, without losing what it learned; the child can also pick how the AI learns.
- **Scope:** `learning.learner`: `AUTO = "auto"`, `algorithm_choices()` (`auto` + the registered names), `describe_algorithm(name) -> (label, description)` in plain words. `learning.promotion`: `promote(table_agent, ...) -> (DQNLearner, agreement)` distils the table into a fresh network (trains on every known row, carries over exploration and counts, remembers the states), `demote(dqn) -> QLearningAgent` fills a table from the network's values for every situation it knows, `convert(agent, algorithm, ...)` picks one or returns the agent unchanged. `DQNLearner.values(state)` and `known_states()`; `resume_from(other)` on both learners. `TrainingService`: default `algorithm=AUTO` (new brains start as `q_table`), `promote_at=DEFAULT_PROMOTE_AT` (1000 known situations); after a go closes under `auto` with a table that large the brain is promoted, announced as a `"promoted"` state change with `last_change` in plain words; `set_algorithm(name)` (idle only) converts the current brain when the choice differs and returns the note. `LearningPanel`: a "How the AI learns" picker (`algorithm_combo`), the status says which the AI is using, promotions and conversions are noted. **Out of scope:** demonstrations (T-707), hierarchical skills (T-708), promoting by anything other than known situations.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `promote` gives a DQN whose greedy move agrees with the table on at least 90% of known situations, knows the same situations, and carries exploration, steps and goes; `demote` gives a table whose rows equal the network's values; `convert` to the same algorithm returns the agent itself and refuses an unknown name.
  2. With `algorithm="auto"` a new brain is a table; once a go ends with at least `promote_at` known situations the brain is a DQN with the same history and counts, a `"promoted"` change is announced once, the saved brain records `dqn`, and a reconnect restores the DQN with no second promotion.
  3. `set_algorithm` refuses an unknown name and a change while training; choosing `dqn` converts a table brain (and `q_table` converts it back) and saves; choosing `auto` keeps the current brain; new brains follow the choice.
  4. The panel's picker lists the choices with plain labels, follows the service, is disabled while training, converts the brain with a note, and the status names what the AI learns with; a promotion is noted.
  5. Every existing training, panel and demonstration test still passes with the new default.
- **Expected changes:** `learning/learner.py`, `learning/promotion.py` (new), `learning/dqn.py`, `learning/qlearning.py`, `learning/__init__.py`, `training/service.py`, `ui/learning_panel.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_promotion.py` (new), `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `tests/ui/test_learning_panel.py`.
- **Test plan:** unit – promote / demote / convert and the labels; service auto-promotion with a small `promote_at`, persistence and reconnect; `set_algorithm` refusals and conversions; panel picker and notes. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 7 / 7 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (4 promotion tests, 2 service tests, 1 panel test; 802 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #55.
- **Completion report:** As planned. Distilling a table into the network showed that the feature vector had too little contrast for small values (lanes 0..2 differed by 1/24), so `FeatureMap` now gives every variable three slots (present, bucket / bins, bucket / (bins + 16)); the T-703 tests were updated for it. `DQNLearner.distil` trains full-batch on every known row and stops early once the greedy moves all agree (the default 64×64 network agrees fully on the test table; a 16-unit one at a high learning rate does not, so the quick test config now uses 0.003). The service promotes under `auto` right after a go closes (before the save), so the saved brain already records `dqn`, and a `"promoted"` state change carries `last_change` to the panel. `set_algorithm` converts either way and keeps exploration, steps and goes. The choices are listed as auto, table, network (the learning order of DESIGN.md §6.1). Known limitation: a demoted table only knows the situations the network had in memory.

### T-707 – Learning from demonstrations
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 7
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §6.3 / §9 "Manual mode": the child's play is kept as demonstrations (inputs, situations, what came of them and the reward) that the AI can learn from again later, so showing the AI a trick helps beyond the moment it was shown. Demonstrating stays optional.
- **Scope:** `learning.demonstrations`: `Demonstrations(capacity=DEFAULT_DEMO_CAPACITY, seed=None)` – a bounded, ordered record of `Transition`s with `source="child"`, `record(...)`, `count`, `__iter__`, `rehearse(learner, *, passes=1) -> int` (feeds every demonstration to `learner.learn` again, oldest first, `passes` times; returns the steps learned), `clear()`, JSON round trip. `EpisodeRunner` gets an optional `on_learned(Transition)` hook called after every learned step with the right `source` ("ai" for its own moves, "child" for observed ones). `Brain` carries `demonstrations` (optional in the saved data; older brains load with none). `TrainingService`: records the child's steps while in manual mode, `demonstrations` property, `rehearse(passes=1) -> int` (idle only), restores and saves them with the brain, clears them on `forget()`, and `summary()` in manual mode counts the moves shown. `LearningPanel`: a "Learn from my moves again" button (`rehearse_button`, enabled when idle and there are demonstrations) and the status says how many moves the child has shown. **Out of scope:** replaying the child's inputs on the emulator, weighting demonstrations above the AI's own experience, importing recordings from files.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `Demonstrations` keeps at most `capacity` transitions (oldest dropped), all marked `child`, round-trips through JSON in order, and refuses bad data; `rehearse` feeds them all, in order, `passes` times and returns the count.
  2. The runner reports every learned step through `on_learned` with the right source; an unstick press is not reported.
  3. In manual mode the service records each learned step of the child's as a demonstration; AI modes record none; the demonstrations survive a disconnect and reconnect through the saved brain, and `forget()` clears them; brains saved before this task load with none.
  4. `rehearse` makes the AI learn from the demonstrations again (its `updates` grow by the count × passes, and a table knows the situations shown) and refuses to run while training; a service without demonstrations rehearses zero.
  5. The panel's button and status follow the service; pressing the button rehearses and notes how many moves were learned again.
- **Expected changes:** `learning/demonstrations.py` (new), `learning/__init__.py`, `training/episode.py`, `training/brain.py`, `training/service.py`, `ui/learning_panel.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_demonstrations.py` (new), `tests/unit/training/test_episode.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_brain.py`, `tests/ui/test_learning_panel.py`.
- **Test plan:** unit – demonstrations bounds, order, JSON, rehearse; runner hook and sources; service recording in manual mode only, persistence, forget, rehearse and refusal; brain round trip with and without demonstrations; panel button and status. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 6 / 6 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (3 demonstration tests, 1 runner test, 1 service test, 1 panel test; 808 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #56.
- **Completion report:** As planned. `Demonstrations` wraps a `ReplayBuffer` so the record is bounded and round-trips like the DQN's memory; `clear()` starts the count again. The runner's `on_learned` hook reports every learned step with its source and the service keeps the child's ones; the AI's own steps are never demonstrations. The Learning panel now also refreshes its status on the child's steps (`TrainingStepped` in manual mode), since those do not come through its own tick. Known limitation: rehearsal feeds the moves to whichever learner is current at its normal learning rate; nothing weights them above the AI's own experience.

### T-708 – Hierarchical learning: a skill per goal step
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 7
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §6.2: the AI learns smaller skills that contribute to larger objectives. Each step of the goal gets its own skill (a learner of its own); the step the AI is working on picks the skill, so "get to the checkpoint" and "finish the lap" no longer fight over one table.
- **Scope:** `learning.skills`: `SKILLS = "skills"`, `DEFAULT_SKILL = "*"` (used when there is no goal step), `SkillsLearner(space, settings=None, *, seed=None, inner=Q_TABLE)` registered as `"skills"`: one inner learner per skill id, created on demand through the registry (`inner` names the kind), `set_skill(id | None) -> Learner`, `skill`, `skills`, `skill_learner(id)`, `adopt(id, learner)`; the `Learner` contract routes `choose` / `greedy` / `learn` / `last_decision` / `epsilon` to the active skill, `updates` and `known` add up, `end_episode` decays every skill, `policy()` merges every skill's policy under a `skill=<n>` part (so insights still parse the keys), `stats()` names the skills, `reset()` drops them; JSON round trip. `describe_algorithm("skills")` = "Skills per goal step". `promotion.convert`: any learner → skills wraps it as the default skill; skills → anything converts its default skill (the other skills are dropped, said in the note). `TrainingService(goals=None)`: with a goal service, the AI's step and the child's observed step first set the active skill to the goal's next step (`None` without a goal or when the goal is done); `AppContext` passes its goal service. **Out of scope:** skills choosing sub-goals themselves, sharing experience between skills, a UI for skills beyond the picker label.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `SkillsLearner` satisfies `Learner`, is registered and labelled, creates a skill per id on demand (the default when none is set), and routes choosing and learning to the active skill while `updates` / `known` add up and `end_episode` decays every skill.
  2. Two goal steps that want opposite moves in the same situation: the skills learner learns both (greedy differs by skill) where one table learns neither reliably.
  3. `policy()` keys parse back through `State.from_key` and tell skills apart; `stats()` names the skills; `reset()` leaves only a fresh default.
  4. A skills learner round-trips through JSON (every skill, the active one, the inner kind, counts); wrong controls, wrong algorithm and a newer schema are refused; the inner kind may be `dqn`.
  5. `convert` wraps any learner into skills (its policy becomes the default skill's) and unwraps the default skill back; `set_algorithm("skills")` on the service works the same way.
  6. With a goal service, the training service switches the active skill to the goal's next step as steps complete, in AI modes and in manual mode, and the saved brain restores every skill.
- **Expected changes:** `learning/skills.py` (new), `learning/learner.py`, `learning/promotion.py`, `learning/__init__.py`, `training/service.py`, `ui/context.py`, `tests/unit/learning/test_skills.py` (new), `tests/unit/learning/test_promotion.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`.
- **Test plan:** unit – conformance and routing; opposite-moves learning vs one table; policy keys, stats, reset; JSON round trip and refusals; convert both ways; service with a goal in AI and manual modes, save and restore. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 6 / 6 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (5 skills tests, 1 service test; 814 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #57.
- **Completion report:** As planned. Skills are saved as an ordered list (the brain store sorts JSON keys, and the order numbers the skills in `policy()`, which the behaviour-change insights compare across goes). `stats()` adds up over every skill. The service test showed the goal service suggesting a new step once the two hand-made ones were done, so the AI keeps working on whatever the goal says is next; without a goal service the general skill plays. Known limitation: a skill starts from nothing when its step is first reached; sharing what the general skill knows with a new skill is a possible follow-up.

### T-709 – Better progress detection on a longer look
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 7
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §16 "more sophisticated game understanding": the first-guess classifier sees a lap counter as a flag while it has only shown 0 and 1, and cannot tell a checkpoint counter from any other slow number. A longer look at how progress-like values move together fixes both: a "flag" that reaches a third value is progress; a value that steps up at the same few places of a position is a checkpoint; a value that steps up each time another starts over counts its rounds (laps).
- **Scope:** `understanding.longlook`: `LongLook(knowledge, *, at_least=Confidence.LOW)` watches believed flags, progress values and positions ("steppers") against believed positions and progress values ("drivers") snapshot by snapshot (`observe`), and on `review()` applies to the knowledge: `third-value` (a flag with three or more values, mostly up → `PROGRESS`), `same-places` (a few-valued stepper that steps up when a wide-ranging position is at the same few places → evidence; a "position" believed so becomes `PROGRESS`, the checkpoint case), `counts-rounds` (steps up each time a driver starts over → evidence; a flag becomes `PROGRESS`). Each evidence kind is added once per stepper / driver. `DiscoveryService` feeds every snapshot to the long look and runs its review with each scheduled review, announcing changed variables in `DiscoveryUpdated.changed`; `summary()` unchanged. Input linking (T-303) no longer turns a progress value into a position once the long look has evidence that it counts (the checkpoint moves with the stick too, because the car does). **Out of scope:** new variable kinds, relationships between events (T-305 has those), anything about scores or lives.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A two-valued "flag" that later shows a third value, mostly climbing, is re-kinded to `PROGRESS` with `third-value` evidence; one that keeps switching between two values is left alone.
  2. A progress value that steps up only when a position sits at the same few places gains `same-places` evidence naming the position, once, and a few-valued "position" doing so becomes `PROGRESS`; a value that steps up at scattered places, and the wide-ranging position itself, do not.
  3. A value that steps up each time another value starts over gains `counts-rounds` evidence, once, and becomes `PROGRESS` if it was a flag; values that step up at other times do not.
  4. Rejected or low-confidence variables are ignored; variables not covered by a snapshot are skipped; `review()` on nothing changes nothing.
  5. On the Toy Racer, after a few laps the long look gives the checkpoint `same-places` evidence against the position and the lap `counts-rounds` evidence against the checkpoint, through the discovery service, announced as changed variables; the first-demo and other tests still pass.
- **Expected changes:** `understanding/longlook.py` (new), `understanding/__init__.py`, `understanding/linking.py`, `services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_longlook.py` (new), `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`.
- **Test plan:** unit – flag to progress and the counter-case; same places and scattered places; counting rounds and not; skipping rules and an empty review; the Toy Racer through the discovery service. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 / 5 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (4 long-look tests, 1 discovery test; 819 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #58.
- **Completion report:** The Toy Racer taught the rules their guards. (1) Input linking (T-303) had been turning the checkpoint into a "position" because the car moves while Up is held; it now leaves a value alone once the long look has evidence it counts. (2) Nearby driver values are one place (`PLACE_SLACK`), the places must cover under a quarter of the position's range, and each place must step the value to the same number nearly every time: a speed ramping up after a respawn at a checkpoint, or a lane changed by a periodic steering pattern, step at the same places but to different numbers, and are not checkpoints. (3) A value that has only ever shown two faces is left a flag whatever else it does: the game-over flag also switches on where the track starts over. With those, the Toy Racer's checkpoint and lap come out as progress with `same-places` and `counts-rounds` evidence through the discovery service. The first-demonstration test now switches off every suggested carrot for its "careful" AI (the AI understands checkpoints and laps now, so it suggests rewards and steps for them) and compares scores per step, since the point-lover now drives fast enough to crash well before the careful one stops.

### T-111 – BizHawk adapter: connect and handshake
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 1 (BizHawk)
- **Objective:** The first slice of the real adapter: open the socket to the bridge script, do the `hello` handshake, detect a missing or wrong-version bridge, disconnect cleanly, and notice a dropped link. Ship the bridge script itself and the two test doubles that stand in for EmuHawk.
- **Scope:** `emulator/bizhawk/protocol.py` (framing: `encode_request`, `decode_response`, `LineChannel` over a socket with a read buffer and timeouts, `Link` with request ids and error mapping); `emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py` (`BizHawkAdapter(host, port)`, name `bizhawk`: `_do_connect` opens TCP with the timeout, sends `hello` with the protocol version, checks the reply, keeps it as `hello`; `_do_disconnect` sends `bye` and closes; `ping()`; socket errors mark the adapter disconnected and raise `EmulatorDisconnectedError`; `_do_game_info` from the hello; capabilities none and no buttons until T-112); `emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua` (the shipped script: LuaSocket server, inline JSON and base64, request loop, `hello`/`ping`/`bye`/`mode`, hold mode pauses EmuHawk and yields, free-run advances; later commands arrive with their tasks); `emulator/bizhawk/simulator.py` (`BizHawkSimulator`: a Python TCP server speaking the protocol over the Toy Racer, with fault switches: no answer, wrong version, garbage, drop after N requests); `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/lua_host.py` (runs the real script under `lupa` as a coroutine with stubbed `emu`, `client`, `gameinfo`, `memory`, `joypad`, `savestate` and a LuaSocket stand-in over real sockets). `lupa` becomes a dev dependency; the script is package data. **Out of scope:** registering the adapter in the default registry (T-117, when it is complete), every other command.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `connect()` to a running bridge completes the handshake: `is_connected`, `hello` holds the script's answer, `game_info()` reflects `system` and `rom`; a second `connect()` is a no-op; `disconnect()` sends `bye`, closes, and is idempotent.
  2. Nothing listening → `EmulatorError` telling the child to open the script in EmuHawk's Lua Console; a bridge that never answers → `EmulatorError` naming the timeout; both leave the adapter disconnected.
  3. A bridge with another protocol version, a non-JSON answer, or a mismatched id → `ProtocolError`, adapter disconnected.
  4. When the bridge closes the socket, the next `ping()` raises `EmulatorDisconnectedError` and `is_connected` is false.
  5. The real Lua script, run under `lupa`, accepts the adapter: hello carries the stubbed system, ROM name, hash and version; hold mode pauses the emulator on hello and unpauses on bye; `mode free_run=true` unpauses; after a disconnect it accepts a new connection.
  6. The script's JSON and base64 agree with Python's on round trips (property-based).
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/{__init__,protocol,adapter,simulator}.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/emulator/bizhawk/retro_ai_lab.lua`, `pyproject.toml` (dev dependency `lupa`, package data), `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/{__init__,lua_host,test_protocol,test_adapter,test_lua_script}.py`, docs.
- **Test plan:** unit – framing round trip and rejections, split packets; adapter against the simulator (handshake, no bridge, no answer, bad version, garbage, drop); adapter against the Lua script under `lupa` (handshake, pause/unpause, reconnect); Lua JSON/base64 vs Python with hypothesis. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 25 / 25 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (6 protocol, 5 adapter-vs-simulator, 14 Lua-script-under-lupa incl. two property tests; 844 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11; Windows CI green after two fixes below). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #60.
- **Completion report:** The real script is tested, not a model of it: `lupa` loads `retro_ai_lab.lua` as a coroutine (EmuHawk resumes scripts the same way), the emulator globals are stubs playing the Toy Racer, and `require("socket")` returns a LuaSocket look-alike over real TCP, so the adapter's handshake, hold mode (pause on hello, no frames while talking, unpause on bye or a vanished app), free-run mode, bad requests and reconnects all run through the shipped file. Two lupa facts shaped the harness: Lua strings are bytes on an `encoding=None` runtime (keys included), and `obj:method()` on a Python object would pass `self` twice, so the stubs are Lua tables calling plain Python functions. Lua tables can't hold nil, so a JSON null inside a container vanishes on the script side; the protocol only uses null in a top-level response field (`image`), and the property test states that limitation. Hold mode's one real-emulator assumption (Lua `frameadvance` moves a paused EmuHawk) is unchanged from T-110 and waits for T-117. Windows CI caught two things Linux hid: a refused TCP connection surfaces as a timeout on Windows (every connect failure now gets the same "Couldn't find BizHawk" advice), and Lua's `%c` class matches bytes of UTF-8 sequences there (`Í` is C3 8D), so the JSON encoder escapes an explicit byte class instead; a regression test pins the exact strings.

### T-112 – BizHawk adapter: capabilities and game info
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 1 (BizHawk)
- **Objective:** Turn the `hello` answer into what the app needs to know about the connected emulator: the game, the buttons, the memory regions and, truthfully, what this bridge can do.
- **Scope:** `emulator/bizhawk/description.py`: `identifier(name)` maps an EmuHawk name (`System Bus`, `P1 A`, `Up`) to the app's lowercase identifier, with collision suffixes; `Description.from_hello(hello)` validates the answer (`system`, `rom`, `hash`, `bizhawk`, `domains` as `[{name, size ≥ 1}]`, `buttons` as strings, `commands` as strings) and keeps `buttons` (`Button(identifier, original)`), `regions` (`MemoryRegion(identifier, size, description=original)`), the name maps both ways, and `commands`; `Description.capabilities(free_run)` derives the flags from the commands the script says it has: `memory_read` needs `read` and a domain, `screen_capture` needs `screenshot`, `input` needs `input` and a button, `frame_advance` needs `step` and hold mode, `speed_control` needs `speed`, `save_states` needs `save` and `load`. The script and the simulator now list their `commands` in `hello`, so capabilities grow as T-113 to T-116 add commands and never claim more than the bridge implements. The script only reports boolean pad entries as buttons (analog axes are not buttons). `connect()` refuses an emulator with no game loaded (`system` `NULL` or an empty ROM name) with "Load a game in EmuHawk first." **Out of scope:** the operations themselves.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `identifier("System Bus") == "system_bus"`, `identifier("P1 A") == "p1_a"`, `identifier("Up") == "up"`; names that collide get `_2`, `_3`; names with no letters get a readable fallback; both maps round-trip.
  2. A hello with the Toy Racer buttons and a 32-byte `RAM` domain gives `buttons()` of seven `Button`s with EmuHawk labels and `memory_regions()` of one region named `ram` with description `RAM`; bad hellos (missing fields, a domain of size 0, non-string button) raise `ProtocolError` from `connect()` and leave the adapter disconnected.
  3. `capabilities()` is all-off for a bridge that lists only `hello`/`ping`/`mode`/`bye`, and each flag turns on exactly with its commands (and `frame_advance` only in hold mode).
  4. Connecting to an EmuHawk with no game loaded fails with "Load a game in EmuHawk first."
  5. The real script under `lupa` reports its `commands` and leaves out a non-boolean pad entry.
- **Expected changes:** `emulator/bizhawk/description.py` (new), `adapter.py`, `simulator.py`, `retro_ai_lab.lua`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/{test_description,test_adapter,test_lua_script,lua_host}.py`.
- **Test plan:** unit – identifiers and maps; description from good and bad hellos; capability derivation table; adapter against the simulator (buttons, regions, caps, no-game refusal); Lua script commands and axis filtering. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 26 / 26 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (identifiers, good/bad hellos, 11-row capability table, adapter-vs-simulator additions, script commands and axis filtering; 870 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #61.
