- **Completion report:** Capabilities are now data from the bridge, not a promise in the adapter: the script lists its command names in `hello`, and each flag needs its command(s) plus the thing it acts on (a button, a domain, hold mode). With only the handshake commands implemented, the adapter reports nothing, so the base class refuses `memory_regions()` even though the regions are already known, which is exactly right until `read` exists (T-115). Names stay EmuHawk's on the wire and become the app's identifiers at the edge, both ways, so later commands can send `System Bus` while the app keeps `system_bus`. An EmuHawk without a game (`system` `NULL`) is refused at connect with a plain instruction.

### T-113 – BizHawk adapter: frame capture
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 1 (BizHawk)
- **Objective:** `capture_frame()` on the BizHawk adapter returns the emulator's screen as a :class:`Frame`.
- **Scope:** Script: `screenshot` command (`client.screenshot(path)` to a file in the temp folder, read back, base64, file removed; a missing file is an error the app can show). Adapter: `_do_capture_frame` asks for `screenshot`, decodes the PNG into an RGB `Frame` numbered with the response's `frame`; a `null` image is an `EmulatorError`, undecodable bytes a `ProtocolError`. `emulator/bizhawk/image.py`: `decode_image(bytes, number)` and `encode_png(frame)` on QtGui's `QImage` (already a dependency; no QtWidgets, nothing from the UI layer; a 256×240 PNG decodes in under a millisecond, where a pure-Python PNG unfilter would take a tenth of a second). Simulator: `screenshot` answers with the Toy Racer's screen as PNG. Test host: `client.screenshot(path)` writes the Toy Racer's screen to the path so the script's file round trip is real. `screen_capture` turns on by itself through T-112's rule. **Out of scope:** capturing inside `step` (T-116), frame rate tuning on the real machine (T-117).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `encode_png`/`decode_image` round-trip grey, RGB and RGBA frames to an RGB frame with the same pixels; bad bytes raise `ProtocolError`; the frame number is carried.
  2. Against the simulator, `capabilities().screen_capture` is true and `capture_frame()` equals the Toy Racer's rendered screen (64×48, 3 channels) with the current frame number; `image: null` → `EmulatorError`; bad base64 → `ProtocolError`.
  3. Through the real script under `lupa`, `capture_frame()` returns the stub's screen, the temp file is gone afterwards, and a screenshot that was not written is reported, not crashed on.
  4. Nothing else changes: the fake adapter and every upper layer keep passing.
- **Expected changes:** `emulator/bizhawk/image.py` (new), `adapter.py`, `simulator.py`, `retro_ai_lab.lua`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/{test_image,test_adapter,test_lua_script,lua_host}.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` §5.
- **Test plan:** unit – image round trips and failures; adapter against the simulator; script under lupa with the real file round trip and the missing-file case. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 / 5 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (3 image, 1 adapter-vs-simulator with three fault cases, 1 script-under-lupa with the real file round trip and the missing-file case; 875 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #62.
- **Completion report:** The screenshot goes through a file because that is the only way EmuHawk's Lua gives pixels to a script without the `comm` socket server: `client.screenshot(path)` into the temp folder, read back, base64'd, removed. Under `lupa` the stubbed `client.screenshot` writes a real PNG of the Toy Racer, so the script's file handling is exercised for real, including the case where nothing was written. Decoding uses QtGui's `QImage` (under a millisecond for a console-sized PNG) rather than a hand-written PNG unfilter that would cost a tenth of a second per frame in Python; `QImageWriter` encodes for the simulator and tests because `QImage.save`'s stubs and runtime disagree about the format argument across PySide6 releases. Frames come out as packed RGB; alpha is dropped. The cost of a screenshot on a real machine (PNG encode plus file plus base64 in Lua) is a T-117 measurement; `comm.mmfScreenshot` remains the fallback in ARCHITECTURE.md §5.4.

### T-114 – BizHawk adapter: controller input
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 1 (BizHawk)
- **Objective:** `set_input(pressed)` on the BizHawk adapter holds those buttons down in EmuHawk until changed, the way the fake adapter does.
- **Scope:** Script: `input` command with `buttons: {EmuHawk name: bool}`; the script remembers the full table and applies it with `joypad.set` before every frame it advances (free-run loop now; `step` in T-116). The pad names come from the handshake, so the script sets every button explicitly (unlisted buttons are released) and the player's own pad does not fight the AI; when the core only exposes prefixed names (`P1 A`) the script sets them that way. A `buttons` that is not a table of booleans, or names a button the pad does not have, is refused. Adapter: `_do_set_input` maps the app's identifiers back to EmuHawk names, sends every button as pressed or not, and keeps `held`; `input` capability follows the command. Simulator: `input` stores the held set (applied by `step` in T-116). Test host: `joypad.set` records the last table and feeds the pressed buttons into the Toy Racer on the next frame. **Out of scope:** frame stepping (T-116), reading the player's own presses (manual mode uses the app's pad, DESIGN.md §9).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Against the simulator, `capabilities().input` is true, `set_input({up, a})` is received as every button with `Up` and `A` true and the rest false, `held` reflects it, and an unknown button is refused by the base class before anything is sent.
  2. Through the real script under `lupa` in free-run mode, holding `Up` makes the Toy Racer speed up within a few frames and releasing (empty set) stops the acceleration; the script sets the whole pad each frame.
  3. The script refuses a non-table `buttons`, a non-boolean value, and an unknown name, each with a message, and stays connected.
  4. Disconnecting clears the adapter's held set, and the script stops driving the pad (so the player's own controller is free again).
- **Expected changes:** `adapter.py`, `simulator.py`, `retro_ai_lab.lua`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/{test_adapter,test_lua_script,lua_host}.py`.
- **Test plan:** unit – adapter against the simulator; script under lupa (acceleration, release, refusals, whole-pad set). Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 2 / 2 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (adapter-vs-simulator with refusals and release; script-under-lupa with acceleration, release, three refusals and the pad left alone after disconnect; 877 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #63.
- **Completion report:** The script sets the *whole* pad every frame (each button true or false) rather than only the pressed ones: `joypad.set` with a partial table would leave the other buttons to the player's controller, and a stray press from a child mid-training would change what the AI learned. The pad names come from the handshake, so both the prefixed (`P1 A`) and per-controller (`A`) naming styles are handled in one place. Once the app disconnects the script stops calling `joypad.set` altogether, which hands the controller straight back to the player (a set only lasts one frame in EmuHawk). The test that proves acceleration runs the real script in free-run mode against the Toy Racer: Up held through the script makes the car speed up, releasing stops it.

### T-115 – BizHawk adapter: memory reads
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 1 (BizHawk)
- **Objective:** `memory_regions()` and `read_memory(region, offset, length)` on the BizHawk adapter return the emulator's memory, so the memory observation and discovery layers work unchanged on a real game.
- **Scope:** Script: `read` command with `reads: [{domain, addr, len}]` answered with `reads: [base64…]`; each read is checked against the domains from the handshake (known name, whole-number address and length, inside the domain) and served with `memory.read_bytes_as_array(addr, len, domain)`, turned into a string in chunks (`string.char` with `table.unpack` has a stack limit). Adapter: `_do_memory_regions` lists the handshake's domains up to `max_region_bytes` (default 256 KiB; the memory observer reads every region whole every frame, and a multi-megabyte bus would swamp a line protocol and the discovery engine alike; `description.regions` still lists everything); `_do_read_memory` sends one read with the domain's EmuHawk name and decodes the answer; bad base64 is a `ProtocolError`, a short answer is caught by the base class. `memory_read` turns on through T-112's rule once `read` exists and a region survives the cap. Simulator: `read` over the Toy Racer's RAM with a `short_reads` fault. **Out of scope:** bundling reads into `step` (T-116), measuring how much memory per frame the real bridge can carry (T-117).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Against the simulator, `memory_read` is true, `memory_regions()` is the 32-byte `ram` region, and `read_memory` returns the Toy Racer's RAM (whole and in part); the base class refuses an unknown region and an out-of-range read before anything is sent; the simulator refuses an unknown domain, bad numbers and a read past the end with messages; a short answer is reported; bad base64 is a `ProtocolError`.
  2. With `max_region_bytes` below the RAM size, `memory_regions()` is empty and `memory_read` is false, while `description.regions` still lists the domain.
  3. Through the real script under `lupa`, `read_memory("ram", …)` equals the stub's RAM, a 64 KiB `system_bus` read works, several reads in one request come back in order, and the refusals have messages.
  4. The memory observation service, pointed at the adapter over the simulator, takes a snapshot that equals the Toy Racer's RAM.
- **Expected changes:** `adapter.py`, `simulator.py`, `retro_ai_lab.lua`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/{test_adapter,test_lua_script}.py`.
- **Test plan:** unit – adapter against the simulator (reads, refusals, cap); script under lupa (reads, batch, refusals); observation service over the adapter. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 2 / 2 passed / 0 failed / 0 excluded (adapter-vs-simulator with the observer, refusals, short answers and the size cap; script-under-lupa with whole, partial, empty, 64 KiB and batched reads plus six refusals; 879 in the full suite, on Python 3.13 and 3.11). Lint, format and strict mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #64.
- **Completion report:** The memory observer reads every region whole on every capture, which is fine for a console's work RAM and hopeless for a 16 MB bus, so the adapter offers only domains up to `max_region_bytes` (256 KiB by default) and reports `memory_read` false if none survive; the handshake's full list stays visible in `description.regions`. The script converts `read_bytes_as_array`'s table to a string 4096 entries at a time because `table.unpack` of a 64 K table overflows the C stack. Reads are a list on the wire already, so `step` (T-116) can bundle them. The honest open question is throughput: a Lua table of 128 K numbers plus base64 per frame on a real EmuHawk is a T-117 measurement, and if it is too slow the observer will need to read only the regions it has learned to care about.

### T-116 – BizHawk adapter: frame advance, speed, save states
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 1 (BizHawk)
- **Objective:** The last three operations of the adapter contract on BizHawk: `advance_frames`, `set_speed`, `save_state`/`load_state`, so the episode runner and the experiment service can drive a real game.
- **Scope:** Script: `step` (`frames` 1..3600; optional `buttons`, `reads`, `screenshot`, so one training step can be one round trip; applies the held pad before each `emu.frameadvance()`), `speed` (`percent` above zero → `client.speedmode`), `save`/`load` (`slot` of letters, digits and underscores → `savestate.save`/`savestate.load` of `<temp>/retro_ai_lab_<slot>.State`, OSD suppressed; `load` answers `loaded: false` when there is no such file). Adapter: `_do_advance_frames` sends `step`; `_do_set_speed` sends the multiplier as a whole percent (at least 1); `_do_save_state`/`_do_load_state`, the latter raising `ConfigurationError("No save state in slot …")` like the fake adapter; `set_free_run(bool)` sends `mode` and flips `frame_advance` off and on. Simulator: the same four commands over the Toy Racer (states kept in memory). **Out of scope:** using `step`'s bundling from the training loop (an optimisation for after T-117's measurements), the real-emulator check that a paused EmuHawk advances on Lua `frameadvance` (T-117).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Against the simulator every capability is on; `advance_frames(3)` moves the Toy Racer three frames with the held pad applied; `set_speed(1.5)` arrives as 150 percent; `save_state`/`load_state` restore the RAM; loading a slot never saved raises `ConfigurationError("No save state in slot …")`; the base class refuses zero frames, a non-positive speed and a bad slot name before sending; `set_free_run(True)` makes `frame_advance` false and the simulator free-running, `set_free_run(False)` the reverse.
  2. Through the real script under `lupa`: in hold mode `advance_frames(5)` moves exactly five frames and the emulator stays paused; a `step` with `buttons`, `reads` and `screenshot` returns the memory and the screen after the frames; `speed` reaches `client.speedmode`; `save`/`load` round-trip through `savestate.save`/`load` with the slot in the file name; refusals (zero, too many or non-integer frames; bad slot) have messages.
  3. The episode runner drives a whole go on the BizHawk adapter over the simulator (step, observe, learn), proving the upper layers are unchanged.
- **Expected changes:** `adapter.py`, `simulator.py`, `retro_ai_lab.lua`, `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/{test_adapter,test_lua_script}.py`.
- **Test plan:** unit – adapter against the simulator (all four operations, refusals, free-run switch); script under lupa (exact frame counts, bundled step, speed, states, refusals); episode runner over the adapter. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 new tests (3 adapter/simulator incl. the episode runner, 2 script under lupa), 2 existing expectations widened; full suite 884/884 on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #65. **PR:** #65.
- **Completion report:** With `step`, `speed`, `save` and `load` in the script the BizHawk adapter now offers every capability, and the episode runner ran a whole go over it (via the simulator) without any change above the emulator layer. `step` applies the held pad before each `emu.frameadvance()` and may bundle `buttons`, `reads` and `screenshot`, so a training step can be one round trip once T-117 measures the real cost; the adapter still uses separate calls, which keeps the observers untouched. Save states live in the temp folder as `retro_ai_lab_<slot>.State` with the slot name restricted to `[A-Za-z0-9_]`, so no request can write outside it. `set_free_run(bool)` on the adapter sends `mode` and drops `frame_advance` while the game runs on its own, which is the fallback if T-117 finds that `emu.frameadvance()` cannot move a paused EmuHawk (ARCHITECTURE.md §5.3). The honest unknowns remain for the real machine: whether frame advance works while `client.pause()` holds the emulator, and what `client.speedmode` accepts.

### T-117 – BizHawk adapter: disconnect handling and real-emulator verification
- **Status:** Done (the real-emulator run is pending on the user's Windows PC)
- **Phase:** 1 (BizHawk)
- **Objective:** Put the BizHawk adapter in the app (the console picker lists "BizHawk (EmuHawk)"), make a silently dead EmuHawk show up as a lost link within the watchdog's interval, and hand the user everything needed to verify the bridge on a real EmuHawk: an opt-in real-emulator test, a setup guide, and a checklist of what to report back. ADR-005's remaining unknowns (frame advance while paused, speed range, path handling) are answered by that run, not by CI.
- **Scope:** `emulator/registry.py`: `default_registry()` registers `BizHawkAdapter`. `emulator/base.py`: `check_link()` hook (not final, defaults to `is_connected`) that the connection service's `check()` uses, so an adapter can probe its transport without sending anything. `emulator/bizhawk/protocol.py`: `LineChannel.peer_closed()` (a zero-timeout `select` and a `MSG_PEEK` read: readable with no bytes means the other end closed). `emulator/bizhawk/adapter.py`: `check_link()` drops the link when the peer closed; the connect failure message names the script's full path so the child can find it. `tests/real_emulator/test_bizhawk.py`: opt-in (ADR-007, `RETRO_AI_LAB_EMULATOR_PATH` = `EmuHawk.exe`, `RETRO_AI_LAB_TEST_ROM`) end-to-end test that launches EmuHawk with `--lua=retro_ai_lab.lua`, connects, and checks every operation plus hold mode; `docs/BIZHAWK.md`: setup and verification guide; README and ARCHITECTURE §5 pointers. **Out of scope:** running the real-emulator test (needs the user's Windows PC; recorded as an explicit exclusion per TESTING_STRATEGY §2.4), VBA-M.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `default_registry()` lists `fake` and `bizhawk`; the connect bar's picker shows "BizHawk (EmuHawk)"; connecting with no EmuHawk running shows the "Couldn't find BizHawk" advice with the script's path in the status line.
  2. With the adapter connected to the simulator and idle, `drop_client()` on the simulator makes `ConnectionService.check()` return False and publish `EmulatorDisconnected(expected=False)` exactly once; a healthy idle link keeps returning True and sends nothing.
  3. `LineChannel.peer_closed()` is False on an open idle socket, False when unread data is waiting, True after the peer closes, and True (not an error) on an already closed channel.
  4. The real-emulator test is collected only when configured (skipped with the recorded reason otherwise) and `docs/BIZHAWK.md` tells the user how to run it and what to report.
- **Expected changes:** `emulator/base.py`, `emulator/registry.py`, `emulator/bizhawk/{protocol,adapter}.py`, `services/connection.py`, `tests/real_emulator/`, `docs/BIZHAWK.md`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, tests.
- **Test plan:** unit – registry and connection service list both adapters; protocol `peer_closed`; adapter `check_link` against the simulator (idle healthy, dropped, already dropped); connection service check over the BizHawk adapter publishes the loss once; UI – connect bar lists BizHawk and shows the script path on failure; real – `tests/real_emulator/test_bizhawk.py` (excluded here). Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 new unit/UI tests pass (registry, protocol probe, adapter watchdog check, script-path advice, connect bar); 5 real-emulator tests written and skipped here with the recorded reason; full suite 888 passed, 5 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean. **Exclusion (TESTING_STRATEGY §2.4):** `tests/real_emulator/test_bizhawk.py` cannot run in this environment (no Windows, no EmuHawk, no ROM); `docs/BIZHAWK.md` carries the manual checklist and the command for the user.
- **Commit:** see PR #66. **PR:** #66.
- **Completion report:** The BizHawk adapter is now in the app: the console picker offers "BizHawk (EmuHawk)" and a failed connect tells the child where the script is. The link watchdog works for a socket adapter through a new `check_link()` hook: the connection service used `is_connected`, which for BizHawk only changed after a failed request, so a crashed EmuHawk would have gone unnoticed in hold mode; the adapter now peeks the socket (zero-timeout select, `MSG_PEEK`) and drops the link when the peer has gone, with the loss reported once as before. What CI cannot answer is written down for the user: `tests/real_emulator/test_bizhawk.py` launches EmuHawk with `--lua`, checks hold-mode stepping (exact frame counts and a still game between steps), reads, screenshots, input, speed, states and the disconnect, and prints timings; `docs/BIZHAWK.md` explains the setup and what to paste back. ADR-005 stays Accepted with that run as its outstanding evidence; the free-run fallback (`set_free_run`) is ready if frame advance turns out not to move a paused EmuHawk.

### E-8 – Expansion: breakdown
DESIGN.md §11 (experiments and comparison), §13 (competition and sharing), §16 Phase 8. The order is chosen so each task is useful on its own: a saved AI is worth having before comparing two, comparing before sharing, sharing before competing. The "AI configuration" of §11 is called a *saved AI* in the app: a name, the rewards, the goal and the brain, saved together per game, so AI A and AI B can be trained on the same game and set side by side. T-801 to T-808 are listed in the summary table; each gets its own section when started.

### T-801 – Saved AIs: a named snapshot of rewards, goal and brain per game
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** The record a child saves as "AI A": its name, the game, when it was saved, a note, and the three parts that make the AI what it is (reward profile, goal, brain), as one JSON file that can be listed, loaded and deleted per game. Everything later in Phase 8 (loading, comparing, sharing, competing) works on this record.
- **Scope:** `comparison/saved_ai.py`: `SavedAi(name, game_id, created, notes, rewards, goal, brain)` holding the parts as the plain dictionaries their own `to_dict` produce (no classes from the layers above storage), `to_dict`/`from_dict` with a schema number, `check_name` (1 to 40 printable characters, trimmed), validation that every part is an object for the same game; convenience properties `episodes`, `total_steps`, `algorithm`, `personality` read from the parts. `storage/saved_ais.py`: `SavedAiStore(folder)` with one folder per game and one file per saved AI (`names(game_id)`, `games()`, `exists`, `save`, `load`, `delete`), refusing a name whose file would collide with a differently spelled one ("AI A" vs "AI_A"), skipping foreign files, atomic writes. **Out of scope:** taking the snapshot from the live services (T-802), comparing (T-803).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A `SavedAi` round-trips through `to_dict`/`from_dict` and through the store unchanged; `created` is an ISO 8601 UTC timestamp made at construction when not given.
  2. Bad names (empty, blank, over 40 characters, control characters) and bad data (a part that is not an object, a part for another game, a newer schema, a missing part) raise `ConfigurationError` with a message naming the problem.
  3. The store lists names per game in alphabetical order, keeps games apart, loads what was saved, deletes, and ignores files that are not saved AIs; a name whose file name clashes with another saved name is refused rather than overwriting it.
  4. The convenience properties read `episodes`, `total_steps`, `algorithm` and `personality` out of the parts and are safe when a part lacks them.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/comparison/{__init__,saved_ai}.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/storage/saved_ais.py`, `storage/__init__.py`, tests.
- **Test plan:** unit – model round trip and validation; store list/save/load/delete/clash/foreign files. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 tests (9 cases with parametrisation) pass; full suite 897 passed, 5 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #67. **PR:** #67.
- **Completion report:** A saved AI is a plain record of dictionaries, checked but not rebuilt, so a file can be listed and validated without a connected game or an action space; T-802 rebuilds the parts through the services that own them. Files carry `"kind": "saved_ai"` so the store can tell its own files from anything else in the folder, and the store refuses a second name that maps to the same file ("AI A" / "AI_A") instead of silently replacing the first. The per-game folder uses the same safe-name rule as the other stores.

### T-802 – Saved AI service: save the current AI under a name, load one back, delete
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** The live counterpart of T-801: while a game is connected, "save this AI as …" takes the current rewards, goal and brain into a `SavedAi`; "load …" puts a saved AI's parts back into the three services that own them, so AI A and AI B can be swapped in and trained in turn; "delete …" removes one. Everything the panel (T-804) needs, with no Qt.
- **Scope:** `comparison/service.py`: `SavedAiService(rewards, goals, training, bus, *, store)` with `enabled` (a game is connected and all three parts exist), `game_id`, `names()`, `get(name)`, `save_as(name, notes="")` → `SavedAi`, `load(name)`, `delete(name)`, and the event `SavedAiChanged(game_id, name, reason)` ("saved", "loaded", "deleted"). `load` refuses while training is running ("Stop training first.") and refuses a saved AI for another game. The services it writes into gain a `replace` entry point that keeps their own invariants: `RewardService.replace(profile)` (same game; rebuilds the exploit detector, clears warnings, saves and announces "loaded"), `GoalService.replace(goal)` (same game; saves and announces "loaded"), `TrainingService.replace_brain(data)` (idle only; rebuilds the brain for the connected game's controls through the same path a saved brain takes on connect, saves and announces "loaded"). `AppContext` gains `saved_ai_store` and `saved_ais`. **Out of scope:** comparing (T-803), the panel (T-804), sharing files (T-805).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Connected to the practice console with a trained brain and edited rewards and goal, `save_as("AI A")` writes a file the store lists, holding the current profile, goal and brain; a second `save_as` with the same name replaces it; `names()` lists both AIs after saving "AI B".
  2. After changing rewards, goal and brain, `load("AI A")` restores all three: the reward values, the goal's objectives, and the brain's episodes/steps/table; the stores on disk hold the loaded parts; one `SavedAiChanged("loaded")` plus the services' own "loaded" events are published.
  3. `load` while training is running, `load` of a missing name, `save_as` with a bad name, and any operation while disconnected raise `ConfigurationError`/`UnsupportedCapabilityError` with a plain message and change nothing.
  4. `delete` removes the file and announces; deleting a missing name returns False quietly.
  5. The three `replace` methods refuse a part for another game and leave the service as it was.
- **Expected changes:** `comparison/service.py`, `comparison/__init__.py`, `services/rewards.py`, `services/goals.py`, `training/service.py`, `ui/context.py`, tests.
- **Test plan:** unit – service over the fake console (save/list/load/delete, replacement of all three parts, refusals, events); each `replace` method directly (same game, other game, idle rule); context owns the service. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 new tests (4 service, 1 context) pass; full suite 902 passed, 5 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #68. **PR:** #68.
- **Completion report:** Wiring the service showed a layering slip in T-801: the store imported the `SavedAi` model from the `comparison` package, which imports the services, which import storage, a circle. The store now works on the saved AI's dictionary like the brain store does (it checks the `kind`, `name` and `game_id` header itself), and the service does the model conversion; T-801's tests were adjusted to match. Loading puts the brain back first, because a brain that does not fit the connected controls is the likeliest refusal, and a refusal then leaves rewards and goal untouched. Each service got a `replace` that goes through its own save-and-announce path, so panels refresh through the "loaded" events they already handle.

### T-803 – Comparison of saved AIs
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §11's comparison: set two or more saved AIs side by side on rewards earned, objectives completed, time taken, number of attempts, successful outcomes and the behaviours they learned, and say in a child's words which did what better, so the child can see why different reward settings produce different results. Pure functions over `SavedAi` records; the panel (T-804) only shows them.
- **Scope:** `comparison/compare.py`: `Scorecard.from_saved(saved)` (name, personality, algorithm, goes = attempts, average and best reward a go, objectives completed, goes that reached a goal and the rate, average steps and frames a go, situations known) computed from the brain's goes through `LearningCurve` and the learner's `policy()`; `AiComparison(cards)` with `leader(measure)` for every measure (ties and empty lists give None), `measures()` listing what is compared with a child-friendly label and whether higher or lower is better; `behaviour_differences(a, b)` → `BehaviourDifference(state_key, a_move, b_move)` for situations both AIs know and would play differently (both learners rebuilt from their dictionaries; different controls are refused); `describe(comparison, knowledge=None, space=None)` producing sentences like "AI A scored more points a go (+12.3 against +4.0) and reached a goal more often (3 of 10 against 1 of 10). AI B finished its goes in fewer steps. In 5 situations they would press different buttons: where Lane=2, Speed=3 AI A presses up and AI B presses up + a." with sensible text for one AI, for AIs with no goes, and for AIs that play alike. **Out of scope:** the panel (T-804), running the AIs under the same conditions (T-806).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A scorecard from a saved AI with known goes reports exactly the numbers in those goes (attempts, average and best reward, objectives, successes, average steps and frames); a saved AI with no goes reports zeros and None where there is no best.
  2. `leader` picks the higher scorer for reward-like measures and the lower for steps and frames, and returns None on a tie or with fewer than two cards.
  3. `behaviour_differences` lists only situations known to both AIs with different best moves; AIs with different controls raise `ConfigurationError`.
  4. `describe` names the leaders per measure, mentions the number of differing situations with one example, and copes with one AI, no goes, equal scores and no differences.
- **Expected changes:** `comparison/compare.py`, `comparison/__init__.py`, tests.
- **Test plan:** unit – scorecards from hand-built goes; leaders and ties; differences from two learners trained apart (and controls mismatch); describe wording; an end-to-end comparison of two AIs saved from the practice console. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 tests pass; full suite 907 passed, 5 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #69. **PR:** #69.
- **Completion report:** Scorecards come straight from the goes a brain carries, through the same `LearningCurve` the learning panel uses, so the comparison and the graph never disagree. "Time taken" is reported as steps and frames a go rather than seconds, because a saved AI may have trained at any speed; the panel can turn frames into seconds at the console's frame rate if that reads better. Leaders are decided per measure with ties and missing values giving no leader, and the sentences name the runner-up with its number ("+6.0 against AI B's +5.0") after the first draft left the losing AI unnamed. Behaviour differences rebuild both learners from their dictionaries and refuse different controls; a DQN's policy is whatever its `policy()` reports, so the comparison works for either algorithm.

### T-804 – "Compare AIs" panel
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** The child-facing side of DESIGN.md §11: a sixth panel where the child saves the current AI under a name ("AI A"), sees every saved AI for this game in one table with the leader of each measure marked, reads the comparison in plain words, watches the saved AIs' learning curves on one graph, and loads or deletes a saved AI. Built on T-802's service and T-803's comparison; no logic of its own beyond presenting them.
- **Scope:** `ui/panels.py`: a sixth `PanelSpec("compare", "Compare AIs", …)`; `education/tips.py`: its "What's this?" tip (simple and advanced). `ui/comparison_graph.py`: `plot_points(..., bounds=)` gains an optional shared value range so several curves share one scale, and `ComparisonGraph(QWidget)` draws one average line (and small dots) per saved AI in its own colour with a legend of names. `ui/compare_panel.py`: `ComparePanel(context)` with a name box and "Save this AI as" button, the comparison table (`QTreeWidget`, one row per saved AI, one column per measure, leaders marked with a star), the description text, the graph, "Load" and "Delete" buttons for the selected row, and a note line for outcomes and refusals ("Give the AI a name.", "Stop training before loading another AI."); refreshes on `SavedAiChanged`, connect and disconnect; everything disabled while no learnable game is connected. `ui/main_window.py`: the panel sits in the bottom row next to Learning; `compare_panel` property. **Out of scope:** sharing files (T-805), running the AIs under one set of conditions (T-806).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The main window shows six panels with "Compare AIs" last; its tip opens from "What's this?".
  2. Disconnected, every control is disabled and the table is empty with the idle text; connected to the practice console, saving "AI A" after two goes lists it with its numbers; saving "AI B" after different goes lists both, marks a leader per measure where there is one, shows the comparison in words and draws two curves.
  3. Loading the selected AI restores it (the training service reports that AI's goes) and says so; loading while training runs is refused in the note and changes nothing; deleting removes the row and says so.
  4. A blank name is refused in the note; the controls follow the selection (Load/Delete disabled with no row selected).
  5. `plot_points` with shared bounds maps the same value to the same y for two curves; the graph widget renders two curves in two colours.
- **Expected changes:** `ui/{panels,main_window,compare_panel,comparison_graph}.py`, `ui/learning_graph.py` (bounds), `education/tips.py`, tests.
- **Test plan:** UI – panel states and actions over the practice console (save, table, text, load, refuse while training, delete, blank name); graph geometry with shared bounds and rendering; main window six panels and the tip. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 new UI tests pass and the main-window test now expects six panels; full suite 912 passed, 5 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #70. **PR:** #70.
- **Completion report:** DESIGN.md §10 names five panels; comparison (§11) earns a sixth in the bottom row rather than a dialog, because the child is meant to keep the table in view while changing rewards and training again. The panel holds no logic: it asks the service for the saved AIs, builds the table from T-803's scorecards and leaders, and shows T-803's sentences. One curve still draws on the graph after a deletion (a single curve is useful context even if it is not a comparison). Loading does not save the current AI first; the tip says so plainly and the name box sits right above the table for that reason. A damaged saved-AI file is skipped by the panel rather than breaking it.

### T-805 – Sharing: export and import a saved AI safely
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §13's first step: a child can hand a saved AI to a friend as one file and load one a friend sent, and "imported projects and models must be handled safely": a shared file can never crash the app, run anything, write outside the saved-AI folder, or quietly replace what the child already has.
- **Scope:** `comparison/sharing.py`: `export_saved_ai(saved, path)` writes the saved AI's dictionary with the app version as `.json`; `import_saved_ai(path, *, max_bytes=MAX_SHARE_BYTES)` reads it back with the checks in order: the file size (refused above 32 MiB before reading), strict JSON (NaN/Infinity refused, must be an object, nesting limited), `kind` must be `saved_ai`, `SavedAi.from_dict` validation, and every part rebuilt through its own `from_dict` (`RewardProfile`, `Goal`, `Brain` incl. the learner) so a damaged part is found at import time, not when loaded; every refusal is a `ConfigurationError` in plain words. `SavedAiService.export_to(name, path)` and `import_from(path)`: the imported AI must be for the connected game; it is stored under its own name, or "name (2)", "name (3)" when that name is taken, never replacing; `SavedAiChanged("imported")`. `ComparePanel`: "Share…" (save dialog) and "Load a shared AI…" (open dialog) buttons backed by testable `export_selected_to(path)` / `import_from(path)` with outcomes in the note. **Out of scope:** merging two AIs, cloud sharing, competitions (T-806).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Export then import gives back an equal saved AI; the file is plain JSON with `kind`, `schema` and `app_version`.
  2. Import refuses, with a plain message and no file written: a missing file, a file above the size cap, non-JSON, JSON with NaN, a list, deeply nested JSON, another kind, a newer schema, a part that does not rebuild (bad learner), a saved AI for another game.
  3. Importing a name that exists stores it as "name (2)" and says so; the existing saved AI is untouched.
  4. The panel's Share writes the selected AI and names the file; Load a shared AI lists the new row and refreshes the comparison; errors land in the note; both disabled while disconnected (Share also with no selection).
- **Expected changes:** `comparison/sharing.py`, `comparison/service.py`, `comparison/__init__.py`, `ui/compare_panel.py`, tests.
- **Test plan:** unit – round trip; each refusal; rename on clash; service same-game rule and events. UI – panel share/load and notes. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 5 new tests (4 unit incl. 12 refusal cases, 1 UI) pass; full suite 928 passed, 5 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #71. **PR:** #71.
- **Completion report:** "Handled safely" (DESIGN.md §13) is read as: a shared file is data checked before it is kept, never code, never a path. The import checks run cheapest first (size before reading, strict JSON with NaN refused, a nesting cap, the envelope) and end by rebuilding each part through its own `from_dict`, so a brain with an unknown algorithm or a reward list of nonsense is refused with a plain sentence at import rather than failing later inside Load. An imported AI never replaces one of ours: it is stored under "name (2)", skipping also names whose file would collide with another saved AI's. The file carries `app_version` for later migrations but nothing about the sender.

### T-806 – Competitions: a challenge and a scoreboard of saved AIs
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §13: "compete on the same game … consistent game versions, settings and starting conditions". Saved AIs race under one set of conditions: the same game, the same starting point (a save state taken where the game is now), the same rewards and goal (the ones currently set), the same number of goes, steps and frames per step, and no learning or exploring during the race (each AI plays its best known move), so the scoreboard reflects what each AI knows rather than luck or a last-minute lesson.
- **Scope:** `learning/greedy.py`: `GreedyPlayer(learner)`, a `Learner` that always plays the wrapped learner's greedy move and ignores `learn`/`end_episode`, so the episode runner can drive it unchanged. `TrainingService.compete(entrants, *, goes, max_steps, frames_per_step)`: idle and enabled only, needs save states; takes the checkpoint, runs every entrant's goes from it (resetting goal progress before each go), puts the game back, and returns each entrant's `EpisodeResult`s; `max_steps` and `harness` accessors. `comparison/competition.py`: `Challenge(goes, max_steps, frames_per_step)` (validated), `Entry(name, results)` with totals and averages, `Scoreboard(challenge, entries)` with `ranked()` (more points a go, then more goal steps, then fewer steps) and `describe()` ("1. AI B: +12.0 points a go, 2 goal steps. 2. AI A: …"), `CompetitionFinished(scoreboard)` event. `SavedAiService.compete(names, challenge)` rebuilds the named AIs' learners, runs them through the training service and publishes the event. Compare AIs panel: "Race them!" button (enabled with two or more saved AIs, idle training) and a scoreboard line. **Out of scope:** running the race off the GUI thread (the practice console is instant; a real emulator at 3 goes × 200 steps is seconds, noted as a follow-up), leaderboards across machines.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `GreedyPlayer` chooses exactly the wrapped learner's greedy move, never explores, and leaves the learner's table and counters unchanged after `learn` and `end_episode`.
  2. `compete` on the practice console with two saved AIs returns the asked number of goes per entrant, each at most `max_steps` steps, from the same starting frame; the training service stays idle with its own episode count and brain unchanged, and the game is back at the starting point afterwards.
  3. The scoreboard ranks by points a go, then goal steps, then fewer steps; `describe` lists the places; the event carries the scoreboard.
  4. Refusals in plain words: fewer than two names, an unknown name, training running, no save states, disconnected.
  5. The panel's race button follows the rules and shows the scoreboard text after a race.
- **Expected changes:** `learning/greedy.py`, `training/service.py`, `comparison/{competition,service,__init__}.py`, `ui/compare_panel.py`, tests.
- **Test plan:** unit – greedy player; challenge and scoreboard by hand; service race over the practice console incl. refusals and the event. UI – race button and scoreboard text. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 4 new tests (3 unit, 1 UI) pass; full suite 932 passed, 5 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #72. **PR:** #72.
- **Completion report:** DESIGN.md §13 asks for consistent conditions, so a race is defined by what is the same for everyone: the save state taken when the race starts, the rewards and goal set now (not each AI's own, which would make the scores incomparable), the goes, the step cap and the frames per step, and no learning or exploring. The last point needed a `GreedyPlayer` that satisfies the `Learner` protocol so the episode runner drives it unchanged; it never touches the wrapped learner, which the tests check by comparing the learner's dictionary before and after. The race runs on the GUI thread: instant on the practice console, seconds on a real emulator for the default three goes; moving it off the thread with a progress note is a follow-up once the real-EmuHawk timings (T-117) are known. Goal progress is reset before each go so every entrant meets the same goal; the game is put back at the starting point afterwards.

### T-807 – Research mode (service): step log, situation values, learning settings
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §16 Phase 8 "Advanced research mode" and §1 "advanced research capabilities": an older child, a teacher or a researcher can see exactly what the AI did step by step, what it thinks every move is worth in the situation in front of it, and change the learning settings (learning rate, discount, exploration) without editing files. This task builds the service layer; the dialog that shows it is T-809.
- **Scope:** `training/research.py`: `StepRecord` (go, step, frame, situation key, buttons, explored, value, reward) and `StepLog(training, bus, *, limit)`: fills from `TrainingStepped` (the go number is the one in progress), keeps the newest `limit` records and counts the ones dropped, clears on a brain from scratch or a loaded brain, `write_csv(path)` with a fixed header. `situation_values(learner, state)` → `ActionValue(label, value, best)` per move; `SituationReport.describe()` in words. `TrainingStepped` gains `step`, `reward` and `situation` (the state the move was chosen in) with defaults, fed from the episode runner's new `last_state`/`last_reward`. `Learner` protocol gains `values(state)` and `configure(settings)`; `QLearningAgent`/`DQNLearner.configure` replace the settings and set the exploration rate to the new starting value; `SkillsLearner` configures every skill and delegates `values` to the active one; `GreedyPlayer` delegates `values` and ignores `configure`. `TrainingService.settings` / `set_settings(settings)` (idle only; applied to the brain in use, saved, announced as "settings"), `current_state()` (the situation now, from the latest memory snapshot). **Out of scope:** the dialog (T-809), per-step logging of DQN losses, logging during races.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. After two goes on the practice console the log holds every step with increasing go/step numbers, the situation the move was chosen in, the buttons pressed, whether it explored and the reward collected; the sum of a go's step rewards equals the go's recorded reward.
  2. The log never exceeds its limit; the oldest records go first and `dropped` says how many; a brain from scratch empties it.
  3. The CSV written has the fixed header and one row per record and reads back with the same values.
  4. `situation_values` lists every move of the space with the learner's value and marks the best; it works for the table, the network, skills and the greedy player.
  5. `set_settings` is refused while training runs and with bad values; afterwards the brain in use learns with the new rate and explores at the new starting rate, new skills inherit the settings, and the settings survive a save and reload.
- **Expected changes:** `training/{research,episode,service,__init__}.py`, `learning/{learner,qlearning,dqn,skills,greedy}.py`, tests.
- **Test plan:** unit – step log over the practice console (contents, limit, clear, CSV round trip); situation values for every learner; settings apply and refusals; the protocol stub gains the two methods. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 9 new unit tests pass; full suite 941 passed, 5 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #73. **PR:** #73.
- **Completion report:** The step log is fed by `TrainingStepped`, which now also says the step number, the reward collected and the situation the move was chosen in (the runner keeps `last_state`/`last_reward`); the older three-field form of the event still constructs, so nothing else changed. The go number is "the go in progress" at the time of the step, which is why the log empties when the brain starts from scratch or is replaced: those restart the count. `values` and `configure` were added to the `Learner` protocol rather than reached through `getattr`, so the type checker guarantees every learner (table, network, skills, the greedy race player, the test stub) answers them. Applying new settings restarts exploration from the new starting rate on purpose: a researcher who sets epsilon wants that rate now, not after the old decay runs out; the setting is saved with the brain so it survives a reload. `current_state()` refreshes the encoder first, as the runner does at the start of a go, so new discoveries show up without a go in between; it answers None once the game is disconnected even though the brain is kept for the reconnect.

### T-808 – Spike: VBA-M feasibility for the second emulator adapter
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §7 names VBA-M beside BizHawk. Find out, from the projects' own sources, whether a VBA-M adapter can meet the adapter contract (ARCHITECTURE §3) and the bridge protocol (§5), and decide what the second real adapter should be.
- **Scope:** Documentation only: ARCHITECTURE.md §6 (what VBA-M v2.2.3, VBA-M's unreleased Lua engine and mGBA 0.10+ scripting each offer per adapter hook, findings, decision) and ADR-010. Sources: VBA-M's README, CMakeLists, `src/wx/lua/` on `master`, the `v2.2.3` tag, the SDL man page's `--gdb` option; mGBA's scripting reference; the `mGBA-http` project; TASVideos' VBA Lua list. **Out of scope:** any code; running an emulator (none available here).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. ARCHITECTURE §6 gives, per adapter hook, what each candidate offers and names the gaps with the API behind each claim.
  2. ADR-010 records the decision with the DESIGN.md §7 objective preserved and the conditions for revisiting it.
  3. The follow-up task is in the summary table.
- **Expected changes:** `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** n/a (documentation). Regression – full suite unchanged.
- **Results:** n/a. Full suite unchanged (941 passed, 5 skipped).
- **Commit:** see PR #74. **PR:** #74.
- **Completion report:** The released VBA-M has no scripting or remote-control route at all; its GDB stub reads memory but can't press buttons, step frames or save states, so an adapter on it is not feasible and the requirement can't be met as written today. VBA-M's `master` has gained a Lua engine since v2.2.3 (frame-advance as a coroutine yield, joypad, byte reads, save states) but it ships no socket library, no screenshot and no range read, so an adapter would need a file or pipe transport and would run without screen capture; it is feasible once released and is kept as a revisit, not dropped. mGBA's scripting API has every call the bridge protocol needs, including sockets, so ADR-010 proposes mGBA as the second adapter (T-810), reusing the §5 protocol and the simulator/lupa tests. Game Boy family games are playable today through BizHawk's cores, so no game is blocked by this.

### T-809 – Research window: learning settings, the situation now, the step log
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §16 Phase 8 "Advanced research mode" and §1: show what T-807 built. A grown-up or an older child opens one window from the toolbar and can change the learning settings, see what the AI thinks every move is worth right now, and take the step log away as a CSV file. It stays out of the six panels so the child's view is unchanged.
- **Scope:** `AppContext.step_log` (a `StepLog` owned by the app, so steps are logged whether or not the window is open). `ui/research_dialog.py`: `ResearchDialog(context)`, non-modal, three groups: *Learning settings* (five spin boxes for alpha, gamma, epsilon, epsilon min, epsilon decay; Apply, enabled with a learnable game connected and training idle; refusals shown in a note), *The situation now* (the state in words and a Move / Value / Best table from `SituationReport`), *Step log* (a count line "N steps logged, M dropped", the newest rows in a table, Save as CSV…, Clear). Refreshes on training state changes and go ends, and on a timer while visible so fast training doesn't repaint per step. `MainWindow`: a "Research" toolbar button opening the window, `research_dialog` property. A `research_mode` tip. **Out of scope:** editing the DQN network config, plotting values over time.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The window shows the live settings; editing and applying them changes `training.settings`; the boxes are disabled while training runs or without a game; a bad combination (epsilon min above epsilon) is refused in words and the live settings stay.
  2. After a go on the practice console the situation table lists every move with a value and marks the best; the words name the situation.
  3. The log count follows the steps; the rows show the newest steps; Save as CSV writes the file T-807 reads back; Clear empties it.
  4. The toolbar button opens the window; closing it keeps logging.
- **Expected changes:** `ui/{research_dialog,context,main_window}.py`, `education/tips.py`, tests.
- **Test plan:** UI – settings round trip and refusals; situation table after a go; log count, rows, CSV and clear; toolbar button. Unit – context owns a log. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 4 new UI tests pass; full suite 945 passed, 5 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #75. **PR:** #75.
- **Completion report:** The window is deliberately off the toolbar rather than a seventh panel, so DESIGN.md §10's child-facing layout is untouched and the six-panel test still holds. The step log is owned by the app context, not the window, so it fills from the first step whether or not anyone opened Research; the test closes the window, trains, and reopens it to see the rows. Fast training publishes hundreds of steps a tick, so the window repaints the situation and the log on a half-second timer while visible (and on state changes and go ends), never per step. A `research_mode` tip was planned and dropped: the education tests pin the topic list to DESIGN.md's named topics, so the window carries its own one-line explanation instead. One test was flaky at first because discovery keeps proposing variables of its own during a go, so the situation's wording can list more than the taught variables; the check now looks for the taught name rather than the whole sentence.

### T-810 – mGBA bridge script
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** ADR-010 / ARCHITECTURE §6: the second real emulator is mGBA. This task is the emulator side only: a Lua script for mGBA's scripting API (0.10+) that speaks the §5 protocol unchanged, so the app's adapter, simulator and tests carry over. As with BizHawk (T-111 to T-116), the script runs in CI inside `lupa` against stubs of mGBA's `emu`, `callbacks`, `socket`, `console` and `C` globals that play the Toy Racer, over a real TCP socket.
- **Scope:** `emulator/mgba/retro_ai_lab.lua`: `socket.bind` + `listen` + `received` events (mGBA's event-driven sockets, polled once a frame), `hello` (script version, `emulator`, `system` from `emu:platform()`, `rom` from `getGameTitle()`, `hash` from the CRC32 checksum, `domains` from `emu.memory` with sizes, `buttons` from `C.GBA_KEY`/`C.GB_KEY`, `commands`), `read` via `MemoryDomain:readRange`, `input` as a key mask applied on every `keysRead`, `step` answered after the asked number of `frame` callbacks (mGBA has no pause from a script, so the game runs between requests: free-run only, §5.3), `screenshot` via `emu:screenshot(path)`, `save`/`load` as state buffers kept by slot name, `mode`, `ping`, `bye`; no `speed` (not in mGBA's API), so that capability is off. `tests/unit/emulator/mgba/lua_host.py`: the stub host. Tests drive the script through the existing `BizHawkAdapter` pointed at the host's port (the protocol is the same; the `mgba` adapter class is T-811). **Out of scope:** the adapter class, registry entry, docs and real-emulator test (T-811); the real-machine checks listed in §6.4.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Handshake: `hello` lists the platform, title, hash, both memory domains with sizes, the ten GBA buttons and the commands (no `speed`); a second connection after `bye` works.
  2. `read` returns the Toy Racer's RAM bytes through `readRange`; out-of-range reads and unknown domains are refused in words.
  3. `input` sets the key mask on every `keysRead`; `step` of N frames answers after exactly N frames with the held keys applied on each, with reads and a screenshot when asked; the step cap holds.
  4. `save`/`load` round-trip through state buffers; `load` of an unknown slot answers `loaded=false`.
  5. The script survives bad JSON, unknown commands and a vanished app, and goes back to waiting.
- **Expected changes:** `emulator/mgba/{__init__.py,retro_ai_lab.lua}`, `tests/unit/emulator/mgba/{lua_host.py,test_lua_script.py}`, ARCHITECTURE §6.4.
- **Test plan:** unit (lupa + TCP) – handshake and reconnect; reads and refusals; input and step timing; screenshot; save states; bad requests and a vanished app; the JSON/base64 codecs agree with Python (property tests). Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 10 new tests pass (8 over TCP against the script under lupa, 2 property tests on the codecs); full suite 955 passed, 5 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #76. **PR:** #76.
- **Completion report:** The script keeps the protocol and borrows the BizHawk script's JSON and base64 code, so the app side needs nothing new; what changed is the shape of the loop. mGBA's scripting is event-driven and has no pause, so the script can't hold the emulator between requests the way the BizHawk script does: it registers `frame` and `keysRead` callbacks and answers a `step` from the frame callback once the asked frames have passed, refusing other requests meanwhile. That makes mGBA a free-run emulator in §5.3's terms, and the reads a step returns describe the frame the answer was built on. The test host mirrors mGBA's model (socket events delivered once a frame, `keysRead` before the frame, `frame` after) over real TCP, with a hold/`run_frames` control so a test can count frames exactly. The real-machine checks are listed in ARCHITECTURE §6.4 for T-811.

### T-811 – mGBA adapter, registry entry, setup guide and opt-in real test
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8
- **Objective:** ADR-010: the child can pick **mGBA** in the console box and play a Game Boy or Game Boy Advance game through the T-810 script, with the same panels and the same training loop as BizHawk; the developer gets a guide and an opt-in test to verify the bridge on a real mGBA.
- **Scope:** `emulator/mgba/adapter.py`: `MgbaAdapter(BizHawkAdapter)` (`name="mgba"`, `display_name="mGBA"`, its own script path and emulator name in messages, `hold` from the handshake). To make that a subclass rather than a copy: `BizHawkAdapter` gets class attributes `script_path` and `emulator_name` used by its connect failure message, and `open_link` takes the emulator's name for "Couldn't find … on port"; `Description` reads `emulator` (falling back to `bizhawk`) for the version text. Registry: `default_registry()` registers `MgbaAdapter` third. Packaging: the mGBA script ships next to its package (spec and package-data). `docs/MGBA.md` setup and verification guide, README pointer, `tests/real_emulator/test_mgba.py` (opt-in: `RETRO_AI_LAB_MGBA_PATH` and `RETRO_AI_LAB_TEST_ROM`; launches mGBA with `--script`, or connects to a running one when `RETRO_AI_LAB_MGBA_RUNNING=1`). **Out of scope:** running the real test (no mGBA here; the user's PC), screenshots through mGBA's own buffer rather than a file.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `MgbaAdapter` connects to the T-810 script under lupa, reports `mGBA` as the emulator, `hold=False`, the right capabilities (no speed control), and drives a training step (`set_input` + `advance_frames`) and the episode runner; the connect failure message names mGBA and the mGBA script's path.
  2. The registry lists `fake`, `bizhawk`, `mgba`; the connection service and the console box offer all three.
  3. The BizHawk adapter is unchanged in behaviour (its tests pass as before; the failure message still names BizHawk and its script).
  4. The guide says how to start a session and what to report; the opt-in test is skipped without the variables.
- **Expected changes:** `emulator/mgba/{adapter,__init__}.py`, `emulator/bizhawk/{adapter,protocol,description}.py`, `emulator/registry.py`, `packaging/retro_ai_lab.spec`, `docs/MGBA.md`, `README.md`, `tests/real_emulator/test_mgba.py`, tests.
- **Test plan:** unit – adapter over the lupa host (handshake, capabilities, step, runner) and over the protocol simulator; registry; connection service names; connect bar offers mGBA and explains a missing mGBA. Regression – full suite including every BizHawk test.
- **Results:** 4 new unit tests pass (5 opt-in real-emulator tests skip without mGBA); full suite 959 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #77. **PR:** #77.
- **Completion report:** The adapter is eleven lines: the BizHawk adapter already read everything that differs (the emulator's name, `hold`, the command list) from the handshake, so making it a subclass only needed the script path, the emulator's name and the connect advice to become class attributes, and `open_link` to take the name it reports. The no-game message now says "the emulator" rather than EmuHawk. `hold` is reset on disconnect so a stale handshake can't describe the next emulator. The real-emulator skip rule was generalised: a module's `real_emulator` marker can name the environment variables it needs, so the BizHawk and mGBA tests are collected independently. The guide tells the child how to load the script in mGBA's scripting window and the developer what the first real run must answer (ARCHITECTURE §6.4); the opt-in test launches mGBA with `--script` and falls back to a running mGBA when that option is missing, since the CLI flag is not in mGBA's scripting reference.

### T-508 – Practise a section: a pinned spot
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 5 (training; DESIGN.md §9 "Checkpoint Training")
- **Objective:** DESIGN.md §9: "use save states to practise specific sections repeatedly: difficult platforming sections, boss battles, racing circuits". Today every Start takes its checkpoint wherever the game happens to be, so after a Stop the next Start begins where the last go ended, and the child has to play back to the hard part each time. With a pinned spot, the child plays to the section once, presses **Practise from here**, and every go and every later Start restarts there until they press **Forget this spot** (or disconnect).
- **Scope:** `TrainingService`: `pin_spot()` (idle, enabled, save states available; saves the game to the `practise` slot and announces "spot pinned"), `unpin_spot()` ("spot forgotten"), `spot_pinned`; `_prepare_checkpoint` restores the pinned spot before taking the per-go checkpoint, so goes restart from it; `compete` restores it after a race too; the pin is dropped on disconnect and on `forget` is kept (it is about the game, not the brain). `summary()` mentions the spot. Learning panel: a **Practise from here** button that becomes **Forget this spot** while pinned, enabled when idle with save states; a note in words. **Out of scope:** several named spots, saving spots between sessions (save states live in the emulator's temp folder; BizHawk and mGBA keep them only while the script runs).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With a spot pinned, `start()` restarts the game from the spot whatever happened since, and each go after the first restarts there too; without a pin, behaviour is unchanged (the tests of T-505 pass as before).
  2. `pin_spot` is refused while training runs, without a game, and without save states (plain words); `unpin_spot` is harmless when nothing is pinned; disconnect clears the pin.
  3. A race (`compete`) leaves the game at the pinned spot afterwards.
  4. The panel's button toggles its text, follows the rules and shows a note; the status says the AI is practising from the picked spot.
- **Expected changes:** `training/service.py`, `ui/learning_panel.py`, tests.
- **Test plan:** unit – pin/unpin/refusals; start restarts from the spot; goes restart there; race restores it; disconnect clears. UI – button text, enablement, note, status line. Regression – full suite.
- **Results:** 4 new tests pass (3 unit, 1 UI); full suite 963 passed, 10 skipped on Python 3.13 and 3.11; ruff and mypy strict clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #78. **PR:** #78.
- **Completion report:** The pinned spot is a second save-state slot (`practise`) beside the per-go checkpoint slot: restoring it before the checkpoint is taken means the existing go-restart logic needs no change, and a race, which takes and restores its own checkpoint, just restores the spot afterwards. The pin is dropped on disconnect because the emulator's slots don't outlive the link (BizHawk and mGBA keep them only while the script runs), and kept across "Start from scratch" because it is about the game, not the brain. The panel button is one toggle with two texts so the child sees one idea; it is enabled only when the console has save states, which is read through the harness. Keeping several named spots and saving them between sessions are out of scope and would need the emulator to export states as bytes. Follow-up on the PR: the UI test for link loss during a go dropped the link after a fixed number of emulator operations, which with an unseeded agent sometimes fell between goes (one CI job failed); it now drops the link three steps into a go, deterministically.
