### T-855 – Guesses that move by themselves are doubted, then dropped
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 23:43 log: in the game at last, every 600-step go earned 15,000 to 24,000 points, far more than Mario earns, because the knowledge holds about 1,600 "score" and "progress" guesses born while the title demo played; the user: "it's getting all it's points while the demo menu screen plays still")
- **Objective:** A score or progress value is something the player earns by playing; a value that changes while nobody presses anything *and* ends up the same whatever is held (a timer, an enemy's position, a music counter, the demo) is not one. Each time the AI asks whether the game listens (at Start and after a stuck or reset go, from a save state) it already plays an idle run and a busy run, so it can see which bytes moved by themselves and did not answer to the pad; a score or progress guess covering such a byte gets a doubt the first time and is rejected by the AI the second time, so it stops earning points and setting goals. A racer's score that climbs while coasting answers to the brake, so it is safe; lives and health are left out because standing still is a fine way to lose them. A confirmed guess is never touched, and a rejected one stays in the Discovery panel where a person can still say "that's right". Known limit: a progress value that advances on its own and ignores the pad (an auto-scrolling level) would be doubted too; the person can put it back.
- **Scope:** `understanding/wakeup.py`: `probe_inputs(connection, buttons, ...)` returns an `InputProbe(matters, self_moving)`; `inputs_matter` wraps it. `self_moving` is the bytes that differ between the save state and the end of the idle run, minus noise (bytes that differ between the two idle runs) and minus the input-driven bytes (those that differ between the idle and busy runs). `DiscoveryService.doubt_self_moving(locations, *, frame=None)`: under the lock, for each unconfirmed, unrejected variable of kind SCORE or PROGRESS whose bytes meet the set, adds `moves-alone` evidence (weight 0) the first time and rejects it (`by="the AI"`) when it already has that evidence; announces the change and logs the names. `TrainingService._listens` uses `probe_inputs` and hands `self_moving` to the discovery service; failures are logged and ignored. **Out of scope:** other kinds (position, speed, flags, counters, lives, health), and guesses the detector would re-propose (a rejected one is kept as a record, so it is not).
- **Acceptance criteria:** on the Toy Racer standing still the probe names the frame counter and timer as self-moving and not the score, lives or position; coasting, the score and position move but answer to the pad, so they are not self-moving; a junk SCORE guess at the timer is doubted after one probe and rejected after the second while the real Score, a confirmed junk guess, and lives and speed guesses are untouched; the change is announced once per probe; at Start the training service runs the check so two Starts reject the junk guess; an emulator without save states skips it; the first-demonstration integration test (a racer whose score climbs while coasting) passes unchanged; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/wakeup.py`, `understanding/__init__.py`, `services/discovery.py`, `training/service.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_wakeup.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_the_probe_names_what_moves_by_itself` (wakeup); `test_self_moving_guesses_are_doubted_then_rejected` (discovery); `test_two_starts_drop_a_guess_that_moves_by_itself` (training); full suite.
- **Results:** the three new tests pass; the T-844/T-848/T-853 training tests now stub or count `probe_inputs` instead of `inputs_matter`; understanding, services, training and integration suites 65 passed; the first-demonstration integration test passes three runs in a row; full suite 1163 passed, 10 skipped.
- **Commit:** see PR #117. **PR:** #117.
- **Completion report:** The first cut doubted anything that changed while nobody pressed anything, and the Toy Racer showed why that is wrong twice over: its score climbs while the car coasts (so a real score can move by itself) and its Lives was the first guess dropped (a life is lost by standing still). The rule became "moved by itself *and* ended the same whatever was held", read off the probe the AI already runs, and it covers only the kinds the player earns, score and progress. The probe now returns a small result instead of a bool so the training service makes one round trip, not two. The log names the guesses it doubts and drops, so the next log from the user will show what it did to the 1,600.

### T-857 – Each go's log line says what earned its points
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 00:38 log: the first Start doubted 22 guesses and dropped 32, Start was found in three seconds with two presses, and the goes earned 3,836 and 2,729 points in 600 steps; far better than 20,000, but the log cannot say whether what is left is Mario's score or more junk, so the next fix would be a guess)
- **Objective:** The "Go N ended by …, +3836.0 points" line names the behaviours that earned the points (the biggest first, with how often each paid), so a log shows at once which guesses the AI is really playing for, and a person reading the Learning panel's history gets the same breakdown.
- **Scope:** `RewardEarned` carries the behaviours' names and shares (what each matched behaviour gave) next to their ids. `EpisodeSignals` adds the shares up per behaviour (and the completed and set-back steps per objective) while listening; `EpisodeResult` gains `earned: tuple[Earning, ...]` (id, name, count, amount, biggest amount first), saved and loaded with the record (an old record loads with none). `describe_earned(earned, most=3)` words it, "Score at ram[0] goes up +2,500.0 (120 times), Progress at ram[4] goes up +900.0 (30 times), and 2 more", and `TrainingService._record` appends ", from …" to the go's log line. **Out of scope:** showing the breakdown in the Learning panel (the record carries it; a later task can show it).
- **Acceptance criteria:** two behaviours matching one event are credited their own shares, not half each; points from a completed step are credited to that step by its description; the breakdown is cleared at the start of a go and ignored while not listening; a result with earnings survives `to_dict`/`from_dict` and an old record without them loads; the words name at most three earners, biggest first, and count the rest; a go's log line ends with "from …" when it earned anything and is unchanged when it earned nothing; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/rewards.py`, `training/episode.py`, `training/service.py`, `training/__init__.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_episode.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_signals_say_what_earned_the_points` and `test_earnings_are_worded_and_saved` (episode); `test_the_go_log_line_names_the_earners` (service); training suite; full suite.
- **Results:** the three new tests pass (plus `test_shares_are_credited_not_split_evenly`); training and rewards suites 87 passed; full suite 1167 passed, 10 skipped.
- **Commit:** see PR #118. **PR:** #118.
- **Completion report:** The signals already heard every reward on the bus, so the breakdown is a dictionary they fill while listening; the only real change of shape is that `RewardEarned` now carries names and shares, which the rewards service had on hand. A goal step is credited under its description, so a line can read "Score some points +50.0 (once)". The multiplication sign the first draft used tripped ruff's ambiguous-character check, so the count is worded ("120 times", "once").

### T-858 – Rewards and steps are offered for the AI's best guess of each role, named by what it does
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's screenshot: the Rewards panel shows 88 rows, seven "Score at ram[…] jumps up" and sixteen pairs of "Losing Lives at ram[…]" / "Gaining Lives at ram[…]"; the user: "not sure how kids will work out whats doing what" and, when offered documented memory maps, "no i want it to learn not read from docs")
- **Objective:** A kid sees a short list in plain words: "Your score jumps up", "Losing a life", "Progress goes up", never an address. The AI keeps every guess, but it offers rewards, goal steps and trigger options only for its best guess of each role (one score, one lives, one health; up to three progress values and three switches, because a game can have a level, a checkpoint and a lap), and it names an unnamed guess by its role, with "(2nd guess)" when two of a kind are offered. A name a person typed in the Discovery panel is always used as typed. The address stays in the Discovery panel and the log, where grown-ups look.
- **Scope:** `Knowledge.offered(at_least)`: the believed guesses narrowed to `OFFERED_PER_KIND` seats per kind (3 for progress and flags, 1 otherwise), keeping the knowledge's order; `plain_names(variables) -> dict[id, name]`: a person's name, else the role word ("Your score", "a life", "Health", "Progress", "Your position", "Speed", "Timer", "Something", "Something"), with "(2nd guess)", "(3rd guess)" for later seats of one kind. `suggest_behaviours`, `merge_suggestions` (goal steps) and `GoalService.trigger_options` use `offered` and `plain_names`. The reward and step templates read naturally with those words: "Losing a life", "Gaining a life", "Your score jumps up", "Progress goes up", "Something switches on", "Get further in Progress". **Out of scope:** the detector, the long look, the state and the exploit watch (they keep the 16 per kind of T-850); folding duplicate rows in the Rewards panel (there are none to fold once only the best is offered); the Discovery panel's wording.
- **Acceptance criteria:** with three unnamed score guesses only the strongest gets a reward row, named "Your score jumps up"; a confirmed guess beats a stronger-looking unconfirmed one; two progress guesses are offered as "Progress goes up" and "Progress (2nd guess) goes up"; a renamed guess keeps its name; the goal steps and trigger options follow the same list; the existing suggestion, goal and panel tests (named Toy Racer variables) pass unchanged; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/knowledge.py`, `understanding/__init__.py`, `rewards/suggestions.py`, `rewards/goals.py`, `services/goals.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_knowledge.py`, `tests/unit/rewards/test_suggestions.py`, `tests/unit/rewards/test_goals.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_goals.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_offered_keeps_one_seat_per_role_and_names_them` (knowledge); `test_only_the_best_guess_of_each_role_is_rewarded_in_plain_words` (suggestions); `test_steps_follow_the_offered_guesses` (goals model); `test_trigger_options_are_plain_words` (goals service); full suite.
- **Results:** the four new tests pass; three T-850-era tests that pinned "16 of a kind" now pin the offered seats (one score, three progress values), and the Rewards panel's rebuild test confirms each new score so it takes the seat; rewards, services, understanding and panel suites 251 passed; full suite 1170 passed, 10 skipped. CI: the Windows py3.12 job failed once in `test_no_bridge_and_silent_bridge` (the port of a closed simulator answered something else, so the connect error was "no 'ok' field" instead of "Couldn't find BizHawk"); a port-reuse race on the runner, outside this change, green on re-run.
- **Commit:** see PR #119. **PR:** #119.
- **Completion report:** The first cut of `offered` handed out seats in the knowledge's order (by id), so the oldest guess won rather than the best; it now takes the seats from `believed`, which already ranks them, and keeps the knowledge's order. The role words were chosen to read inside the existing templates ("Losing a life", "Your score jumps up", "Get further in Progress") so no template changed. Known limit: a game with two real scores (a player's and a bonus) gets a reward for one of them until a person names the other, which confirms it and gives it the seat.

### T-860 – A person can add a reward for any value going up or down
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user: "how do i add something like progressing x pixels position + is positive and - is negative?")
- **Objective:** A reward of the person's own on any value the AI knows: "Your position goes up: +2" and "Your position goes down: -2". The AI then earns (or loses) those points every step the value moves that way, the rows survive the AI's own suggestions and a reconnect, and a person can remove them. The panel controls come in T-861; this task is the model, the detector and the service.
- **Scope:** `EventKind.UP` / `EventKind.DOWN`: a value the person asked about went up or down (any amount), emitted by the `EventDetector` only for *asked* variables (`ask(var_ids)`), which it tracks whatever their kind or confidence as long as they are not rejected; the cached watch list follows the asked set. `rewards/own.py`: `own_behaviour(var, name, goes_up, value)` builds `Behaviour("mine:up:<id>" | "mine:down:<id>", "<name> goes up|down", PROGRESSION, Trigger(UP|DOWN, id), custom=True, notes="You added this.")`; the `mine:` prefix is not an event kind, so `apply_suggestions` leaves the row alone. `DiscoveryService.ask_for(var_ids)` keeps the set and gives it to the detector (and to a new detector on connect or import). `RewardService.add_own(variable_id, *, goes_up, value, name=None)` (the name defaults to the plain name of an offered guess, else the display name; a rejected or unknown value, or a row already there, is a `ConfigurationError`), `remove_own(behaviour_id)` (only a `mine:` row), `own()`; after loading a profile and after every add or remove the service asks the discovery service for the variables of every `mine:` row. **Out of scope:** the panel (T-861); a reward per unit moved (one event per step the value moves is enough for learning; noted as a possible later option).
- **Acceptance criteria:** an asked value emits UP and DOWN with before/after on every change and nothing while still; an unasked value of a kind the detector does not track emits nothing; asking after the first snapshot starts tracking without a false event; a lives value that is asked emits both its SETBACK and a DOWN; `own_behaviour` names and keys the row and `is_automatic` is false for it; the service adds the row, the detector emits on the Toy Racer's lane moving, the reward is earned with the row's name, the row survives `apply_suggestions` and a disconnect/reconnect, and `remove_own` takes it out and stops asking; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/events.py`, `rewards/own.py`, `rewards/__init__.py`, `services/discovery.py`, `services/rewards.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_events.py`, `tests/unit/rewards/test_own.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_rewards.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_asked_values_report_every_move` (events); `test_an_own_reward_is_built_and_kept` (own); `test_a_person_adds_a_reward_on_a_value_going_up_or_down` (rewards service); full suite.
- **Results:** the three new tests pass; events, rewards, rewards-service and discovery suites 92 passed; full suite 1175 passed, 10 skipped. CI: the Windows py3.11 job failed once in `test_handshake_hold_mode_and_reconnect` (the Lua test that flaked under load in T-853 too; this change does not touch the Lua script or the adapter), green on re-run; making that test deterministic is worth its own task.
- **Commit:** see PR #121. **PR:** #121.
- **Completion report:** The detector already decodes every tracked value each step, so asked values ride the same loop; the only care was the cached watch list, which is now keyed on the asked set as well as the knowledge version and the regions, and a value that stops being asked about leaves the watch list the same way one that loses its seat does. The `mine:` id prefix keeps `apply_suggestions` (which removes automatic rows it no longer suggests) away from a person's rows without a new flag.

### T-862 – A memory view in the Research window, with naming
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user: "like a simple hex ide"; and "i want it to learn not read from docs", so the view shows what the AI worked out and lets a person name a value, never a documented map)
- **Objective:** Grown-ups get a table of the game's memory in the Research window: every address whose byte behaves like game state (and every address the AI has a guess at), its value now, how often it changes, what the AI thinks it is and how sure, and the name a person gave it; a filter box narrows it, and a name box names the picked address (the AI's guess there is confirmed under that name, or a new named value is made where it had none), which every kid-facing list then uses.
- **Scope:** `DiscoveryService.name_value(region, offset, name)`: `correct` the variable at that address if there is one, else `propose` one (kind unknown, a weight-0 "Named by a person." evidence) and `correct` it; saved and announced as changed or added. `ResearchDialog`: a "Memory" group with `memory_summary`, `memory_filter`, `memory_table` ("Address", "Now", "Changes", "The AI thinks", "Name"; at most `MAX_MEMORY_ROWS` = 400 rows, by address), `memory_name_edit` and `memory_name_button`; accessors `memory_rows()`, `select_memory(address)`, `set_memory_filter(text)`, `name_memory(name)`; refreshed with the rest of the live view (twice a second while open) keeping the picked address. **Out of scope:** editing memory (the design keeps the sandbox read-only for children), a raw hex grid of every byte (the filter plus state-only rows is the readable version; a full grid can come later if asked).
- **Acceptance criteria:** with the Toy Racer connected and a few steps played, the table lists the bytes classed as state and the AI's guesses with their values and badges; the filter narrows by address or name; naming a picked address with a guess renames and confirms it (the Rewards panel then calls it by that name); naming an address with no guess makes a confirmed named value there; an empty name is refused; disconnected the table is empty and the summary says so; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `ui/research_dialog.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `tests/ui/test_research_dialog.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_person_names_a_value_by_address` (discovery service); `test_memory_view_lists_names_and_filters` (research dialog); full suite.
- **Results:** both new tests pass; discovery and Research dialog suites 23 passed; full suite 1178 passed, 10 skipped on the second run. The first run had one failure, `test_handshake_hold_mode_and_reconnect` (the Lua test that flaked in T-853 and on the T-860 CI run; this change does not touch the Lua script or its host): its last assertion reads the host's call list at a moment that depends on the host thread's timing. Fixed as T-863, not hidden.
- **Commit:** see PR #123. **PR:** #123.
- **Completion report:** Everything the table shows was already in the app (the snapshot, the change tracker's statistics, the knowledge); the view only joins them by address. Naming went into the discovery service rather than the dialog so that the Discovery panel can use the same path later and so that the rewards, steps and lists hear about it on the bus. Rows are capped and filtered rather than paged: on Super Mario Bros. the tracker classes a few hundred bytes as state, which fits.

### T-863 – The Lua host test no longer depends on the host thread's timing
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (`test_handshake_hold_mode_and_reconnect` failed once under load in T-853, once on the T-860 Windows CI run and once in the T-862 full suite, each time with `assert ['joypad_get', 'unpause'] == ['unpause', 'displaymessages']`)
- **Objective:** The test checks what the script does when the app disconnects (release the pad, unpause, bring the messages back) without reading the host's call list at a moment that depends on the host thread.
- **Scope:** The assertion waited for `not host.paused`, which the host sets inside `unpause`, and then read the last two calls; between `unpause` and `displaymessages` the main thread can run first, and `release_all` reads the pad just before `unpause`, so the tail was sometimes `['joypad_get', 'unpause']`. The test now marks the call list before the disconnect, waits until `displaymessages` has been recorded after the mark, and asserts that the hand-back calls after the mark are `unpause` then `displaymessages` in that order, with the emulator unpaused. Nothing in the script or the host changes. **Out of scope:** the other Lua tests (they wait on frames or answers, not on the call list).
- **Acceptance criteria:** the test passes 20 runs in a row alone and 10 under the full suite's load; the same check still fails if the script does not unpause or does not bring the messages back (checked by hand against a copy of the script with the `unpause()` line removed, then restored).
- **Expected changes:** `tests/unit/emulator/bizhawk/test_lua_script.py`, `tests/ui/test_research_dialog.py` (found on the PR's CI run), `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** the test itself, 20 solo runs, 10 loaded runs; full suite.
- **Results:** 20 solo runs and 10 runs under the full suite's load all pass; the full suite running alongside: 1178 passed, 10 skipped. The PR's Windows py3.11 run then failed `test_memory_view_lists_names_and_filters` (T-862) with `StopIteration`: after two random training steps the AI had a guess at every state byte, so the test found no bare address to name. The test now takes one guess away when that happens (the address is then exactly as it is before the AI proposes it) and names that; both paths checked by hand, 8 runs pass. With `unpause()` removed from the script's `drop_app` the test fails on the order (`['displaymessages'] == ['unpause', 'displaymessages']`); with `show_messages(true)` removed it fails on the wait ("condition not met in time"); the script was restored unchanged.
- **Commit:** see PR #124. **PR:** #124.
- **Completion report:** The old assertion was right about the script and wrong about when to look: `unpause` flips the flag the test waited on, and the second call had not always landed by the time the main thread read the list. Waiting for the last call of the hand-back and then checking the order is the same claim, made at a moment that does not depend on the scheduler.

### T-864 – A life count that bounces is not lives
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 09:47 log, on the build with readable rewards: each 600-step go ended around -900 points, "from Losing a life -2,320.0 (116 times), Gaining a life +512.5 (101 times), Progress (2nd guess) goes up +305.0 (61 times)"; a life lost 116 times and regained 101 times in one go is not a life count)
- **Objective:** The AI's lives rule ("nearly only goes down, through a handful of values") is judged once, when the byte is first proposed, on whatever it had seen by then. A byte that later turns out to go up nearly as often as it goes down (an enemy's slot, a bouncing gauge, a menu cursor) is not a life count, and every move of it was paying or costing points. At each review the AI now reads the fresh change statistics of every unconfirmed LIVES guess: one that has gone up at least `BOUNCE_UPS` (4) times and down fewer than twice that often gets a doubt (`bounces` evidence, weight 0) the first time and is rejected by the AI at the next review, the way T-855 drops self-moving scores; so within two reviews (240 observed steps) the bogus reward is gone. A real life count keeps the rule: lives are lost one at a time and restored several at once on a restart, so its downs stay at least double its ups (the Toy Racer after 600 frames: 20 down, 6 up). A confirmed or named guess is never touched; a rejected one stays in the Discovery panel where a person can put it back. Health is left alone (it is meant to go both ways). Known limit: a game where a save state is loaded after every single life lost would show one up per down and be doubted too; the person can confirm the real one in the Memory table.
- **Scope:** `understanding/variables.py`: `BOUNCE_UPS = 4` and `lives_bounce(st: ByteStats) -> bool` (ups at least `BOUNCE_UPS` and downs under twice the ups), exported. `services/discovery.py`: `BOUNCES = "bounces"` evidence kind; `DiscoveryService.doubt_bouncing(*, frame=None) -> tuple[Variable, ...]` under the lock: for each unconfirmed, unrejected LIVES guess whose first byte the tracker has statistics for and `lives_bounce` says bounces, add `bounces` evidence the first time and reject (`by="the AI"`) when it already has it; log the names like T-855 and announce the changes; `review()` calls it after proposing candidates, so the long look sees the verdict. **Out of scope:** re-proposing the byte as something else (a rejected guess is a record, as in T-855), health and other kinds, and the reward values themselves.
- **Acceptance criteria:** `lives_bounce` is false for a byte that never went up, for one with fewer than four ups, and for a life count restored by restarts (20 down, 6 up), and true for one that went up and down alike; on the Toy Racer a LIVES guess planted on the lane byte (which weaves left and right as much) is doubted at one review and dropped at the next while the real Lives guess on the lives byte and a confirmed LIVES guess on the lane byte are untouched; the drop is announced and logged; calling it with no knowledge or no bouncing guess changes nothing; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `understanding/__init__.py`, `services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_life_count_restored_by_restarts_does_not_bounce` (variables); `test_a_lives_guess_that_bounces_is_doubted_then_dropped` (discovery); full suite.
- **Results:** both new tests pass; understanding and discovery suites 29 passed; full suite 1180 passed, 10 skipped on the second run. The first run (89 s on a slow machine, against 48 s usual) had one failure in `test_gui_stays_responsive_while_connecting` (`assert 1 >= 5` event-loop ticks during a 0.4 s connect): a load-sensitive timing test that this change does not touch; it passed three solo runs and the second full run.
- **Commit:** see PR #125. **PR:** #125.
- **Completion report:** The lives rule was right about the byte when it was proposed and nobody ever looked again, so a byte that started out dropping and then bounced for the rest of the go kept a reward on every move. The fix is the smallest honest one: at each review the AI re-reads the byte's statistics against a rule a life count cannot break (restored at most half as often as lost), and uses the same doubt-then-drop verdict as the self-moving scores. The log line will now name the drop ("1 life guesses go up nearly as often as down: ... dropped"), so the user's next log shows whether the remaining points are Mario's.

### T-865 – A life count changes rarely
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 10:13 log, on the T-864 build: "Go 20 ended by max_steps after 600 steps, -3215.0 points, from Losing a life -4,391.2 (496 times)"; the byte now only drops, 496 times in 600 steps, so the bounce rule never fires, and a life lost 496 times a minute is not a life count either)
- **Objective:** A life count is lost rarely: a few times a go, not nearly every step. The lives rule in the classifier lets a byte through that "changes sometimes" (weight 0.5 either way) as long as it mostly drops through a handful of values, and a sub-counter that ticks down 5, 4, 3, 2, 1 and resets looks exactly like that. Two changes: the classifier only proposes LIVES for a byte that changes rarely (`RARE_RATE`, under one change in ten observed steps; otherwise it falls through to the gauge and unknown rules), and the review re-check of T-864 gets a second reason: a LIVES guess whose byte, after at least `LIVES_SAMPLES` (100) observed steps, changes more often than `RARE_RATE` is "busy", doubted at one review and dropped at the next, with the same safeguards (confirmed or named guesses untouched, rejected ones kept as a record). The Toy Racer's lives under a child's driving change about 4 times in 100 steps, so they stay.
- **Scope:** `understanding/variables.py`: `LIVES_SAMPLES = 100`, `lives_busy(st)` (enough samples and `change_rate > RARE_RATE`), `lives_doubt(st) -> str | None` returning "bounces" / "busy" / None; the LIVES branch of `_classify_single` requires `rare`. `services/discovery.py`: `BUSY = "busy"` evidence kind; `doubt_bouncing` uses `lives_doubt`, writes `bounces` or `busy` evidence with the matching sentence, and counts a guess already carrying either as its second strike; the log line reads "N life guesses change like no life count does". **Out of scope:** other kinds; re-proposing the byte as something else.
- **Acceptance criteria:** `lives_busy` is false under 100 samples and for the Toy Racer's lives (26 changes in 600), true for the log's byte (496 in 600); `lives_doubt` names the reason; a byte that drops through five values nearly every step is no longer proposed as LIVES; on the Toy Racer a LIVES guess planted on the position byte (which changes every step) is doubted at one review and dropped at the next, the lane guess still falls for bouncing, and the real Lives and a confirmed guess are untouched; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `understanding/__init__.py`, `services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_life_count_changes_rarely` (variables); `test_a_lives_guess_that_bounces_is_doubted_then_dropped` extended with the busy byte (discovery); full suite.
- **Results:** the new test and the extended one pass; understanding and discovery suites 30 passed; full suite 1181 passed, 10 skipped. The "busy" reason is checked before "bounces" (the Toy Racer's position byte is both, and "changes rarely" is the simpler claim), so the T-864 log case (217 changes in 600 steps) now reads as busy; the lane byte (20 changes in 600) still falls for bouncing.
- **Commit:** see PR #126. **PR:** #126.
- **Completion report:** T-864 caught a byte that bounced and missed one that only ever drops, because the lives rule had never asked how often. "A life count changes rarely" is the rule a person would have stated first; it now holds both when a byte is proposed and at every review after. The two reasons share one doubt-then-drop path, so a byte that fails either way is gone within two reviews.

### T-866 – Progress changes rarely too
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 10:30 log, on the T-865 build: the bogus lives guesses were dropped within two reviews and the goes now earn +2,000 to +3,000, "from Progress goes up +1,720.0 (117 times), Progress (3rd guess) goes up +690.0 (61 times), Progress (2nd guess) goes up +520.0 (52 times)"; a level, lap or checkpoint does not step up 117 times in 600 steps)
- **Objective:** The same thing that fooled the lives rule fools the progress rule: the classifier asks for a rarely changing byte when it proposes progress and never again, so a byte that was rare on the title screen and busy in the game keeps a reward on every step up. The review re-check of T-864/T-865 now covers PROGRESS guesses with a rule of their own: "busy" is the same (more than one change in ten steps after 100 observed steps); "bounces" for progress means the byte has gone down at least as often as up, with at least `BOUNCE_UPS` ups (progress only ever comes down on a restart, so the Toy Racer's lap, 4 up and 3 down under a crashing child, stays). Same two-step verdict, same safeguards: confirmed or named guesses untouched, a rejected one kept as a record a person can put back. Known limit: a progress value that advances every few seconds for a whole minute (a fast auto-scroller's screen counter) would be dropped; a person can confirm it in the Memory table.
- **Scope:** `understanding/variables.py`: `progress_doubt(st) -> str | None` ("busy" via the shared check, "bounces" when `ups >= BOUNCE_UPS and downs >= ups`), exported. `services/discovery.py`: `KIND_DOUBTS` maps LIVES and PROGRESS to their doubt function and plain name ("a life count" / "progress"); `doubt_bouncing` loops over both kinds, words the evidence with the kind's name, and logs "N guesses change like no life count or progress does". **Out of scope:** scores (they go up often by nature), other kinds, re-proposing the byte.
- **Acceptance criteria:** `progress_doubt` is None for the Toy Racer's checkpoint (27 up, 9 down in 600) and lap (4 up, 3 down), "busy" for the log's byte (117 ups in 600 steps) and "bounces" for a byte that goes down as often as up; on the Toy Racer a PROGRESS guess planted on the position byte is doubted at one review and dropped at the next, the real checkpoint and lap guesses are untouched; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `understanding/__init__.py`, `services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_progress_changes_rarely_too` (variables); `test_a_lives_guess_that_bounces_is_doubted_then_dropped` extended with a progress guess (discovery); full suite.
- **Results:** the new test and the extended one pass; understanding and discovery suites 31 passed; full suite 1182 passed, 10 skipped.
- **Commit:** see PR #127. **PR:** #127.
- **Completion report:** The third log in a row made the same point about a different kind: the classifier judges a byte once, and a title screen is a quiet place to be judged. The re-check is now a small table of kinds and rules rather than a lives-only method, so the next kind that needs one is a line, not a task. Scores are deliberately left out: they go up often by nature, and T-855 already drops the ones that move by themselves.

### T-867 – Flags switching back are not a restart
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 10:51 log, on the T-866 build: the busy progress guesses were dropped as meant, and then 88 goes in six minutes, every one "ended by reset after 7 to 80 steps" with "Flag at ram[2], Flag at ram[1793] all went back to their starting values"; the goes earned nothing but "Starting over -5" and the AI never got to play)
- **Objective:** With the junk guesses gone, only a handful of values are watched, so two flags flipping back to their first value are "most of what has moved" (T-846), one of them is fairly sure, and a flag going 1 to 0 counts as the drop T-838 asks for. But a flag switching back is what flags do (a door closes, a jump ends); a restart is a number going back down to where it began: score, lives, progress. Two changes: (1) the detector's "something dropped" condition now ignores flags, so a reset needs at least one non-flag value going home with a drop, while flags still count toward the share and the names; (2) T-845's "same values ended three goes in a row" rule looked only at goes of at most 3 steps, and these ran 7 to 80, so it never fired; `RESET_SUSPECT_STEPS` is now 120 (half a minute of play), so three goes in a row cut short by the same group within that teach the AI those values cycle, whatever they are. Known limit: a game the AI really loses three times in a row inside 30 seconds, with the same values going home, would have its restart doubted; the only cost is that goes then run to their step limit instead of ending at the game over, and the Discovery panel shows the `cycles` evidence a person can judge.
- **Scope:** `understanding/events.py`: in `observe`, `dropped` is set only by a value whose kind is not FLAG. `training/service.py`: `RESET_SUSPECT_STEPS = 120`. **Out of scope:** the share rule itself (T-846), re-kinding the flags, the "Starting over" reward.
- **Acceptance criteria:** two flags switching back together, with a progress value still away from home, is no reset; a progress value dropping home alongside a flag is; every earlier reset test passes unchanged; the service's cycle rule fires for three goes of 100 steps ended by the same group and the constant is 120; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/events.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_events.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_flags_switching_back_are_not_a_restart` (events); `test_three_short_resets_from_the_same_values_teach_the_ai_they_cycle` extended to check the wider window (service); full suite.
- **Results:** the new test and the extended one pass; events and training-service suites 58 passed (every earlier reset test unchanged); full suite 1183 passed, 10 skipped.
- **Commit:** see PR #128. **PR:** #128.
- **Completion report:** Each rule that pruned the knowledge made the reset test easier to pass by accident, because "half of what has moved" shrinks with the watch list. The fix is to ask what a restart actually is, a number going back down to where it began, rather than to tune the share again; and the cycle rule's three-step window, right for the 1-to-2-step resets of T-845, now covers the 7-to-80-step ones this log showed.

### T-868 – No doubts while the game is deaf
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 11:16 log, on the T-867 build: goes run their full 600 steps at last, but every one earns only "Something (3rd guess) switches on" and nothing else; no score, no progress, no lives are left. Each session opens with "N guesses changed while nobody pressed anything" *before* "The game is not listening to the AI; trying each button", so the T-855 doubt has been judged on the title demo every time)
- **Objective:** T-855's rule is "moved by itself *and* ended the same whatever was held". While a title demo plays, that is true of everything, including the real score the demo's player earns, so each Start on the demo doubted it and the next one dropped it, and a rejected guess is never re-proposed; over a morning of sessions the saved knowledge lost every real value. The probe already says whether the game listens (`InputProbe.matters`); the doubt is now issued only when it does. When the game is deaf, the search for the wake button runs as before and nothing is doubted. The knowledge already poisoned is not repaired by code: "Start from scratch" in the Learning panel forgets it, and the README says so.
- **Scope:** `training/service.py`: `_listens` calls `doubt_self_moving` only when `probe.matters`. `README.md`: a line under the rewards section about "Start from scratch" after a long run on an old build. **Out of scope:** un-rejecting guesses the AI dropped on earlier builds (a person can confirm them in the Memory table or start from scratch); the busy flag reward (next task).
- **Acceptance criteria:** with the probe stubbed to "the game does not listen", a junk guess on a self-moving byte gets no `moves-alone` evidence at Start; with the probe stubbed to "it listens", the same guess is doubted; the T-855 test passes unchanged; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_deaf_game_doubts_nothing` (service); full suite.
- **Results:** `test_a_deaf_game_doubts_nothing` passes (deaf probe: no `moves-alone` evidence; listening probe: doubted); service suite 41 passed; full suite 1184 passed, 10 skipped; ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #129. **PR:** #129.
- **Completion report:** `_listens` returns False before touching the discovery service when the probe says the game ignores the pad, so the title demo can no longer teach the AI that the real score "moves by itself". The README tells a person who ran older builds to press "Start from scratch" once so the poisoned knowledge is forgotten.

### T-869 – A flag blinking every few steps is not an event
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 11:16 log, on the T-867 build: every full-length go earned only "Something (3rd guess) switches on +210.0 (70 times)", a two-value byte toggling about every eight steps: an animation or a "jumping" bit, not a game over, a menu or a pause)
- **Objective:** T-865 and T-866 taught the AI that a life count and progress change rarely. The same holds for the flags it rewards: switching on and off 140 times in 600 steps is how an animation counter looks, not an event. A flag guess that changes more than once in ten steps (after 100 observed steps, as for lives) is now doubted at one review and dropped by the AI at the next, through the same `KIND_DOUBTS` table, and the classifier no longer proposes a flag for a two-value byte that is busy (it proposes nothing for it, as the SCORE and SPEED fall-throughs fit it even less). Generic, as everything in this chain: no game is named.
- **Scope:** `understanding/variables.py`: `flag_doubt` ("busy" or None); both FLAG branches of `_classify_single` require `rare` and return None otherwise. `services/discovery.py`: `KIND_DOUBTS[FLAG] = (flag_doubt, "a flag")`; log line names flags. **Out of scope:** a flag that stays on for minutes (a "level done" bit) is still fine; rewards of the person's own on any value are untouched.
- **Acceptance criteria:** `flag_doubt` returns "busy" for the log's byte (samples 600, changes 140) and None for a rare one and before 100 samples; a two-value byte toggling every eight steps gets no guess from the classifier while one switching twice in 400 steps is still a FLAG; a planted FLAG guess on a busy byte is doubted at one review and dropped at the next, a rare FLAG guess untouched; the T-864 to T-866 tests pass unchanged; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/variables.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/understanding/__init__.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `tests/unit/understanding/test_variables.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_flag_switches_rarely` (variables); `test_a_busy_flag_guess_is_doubted_then_dropped` (discovery); full suite.
- **Results:** both new tests pass; the T-864 to T-866 tests pass with only the log wording updated ("no life count, progress or flag"); full suite 1186 passed, 10 skipped; ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #130. **PR:** #130.
- **Completion report:** `flag_doubt` joins `KIND_DOUBTS`, so a flag guess changing more than once in ten steps is doubted at one review and dropped by the AI at the next; both FLAG branches of the classifier now require `rare` and propose nothing for a blinking bit.

### T-870 – Values that move while the AI plays get the seats
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 8 (the user's 11:52 log, on the T-869 build after "Start from scratch": the goes run full length and the AI collected coins, but the user expected the coin count, once it went up, to be recognised and offered in the behaviours so they could reward it)
- **Objective:** Rewards and steps are offered for the best guess of each role (T-858), three seats for progress, by confirmation, confidence, evidence weight and then address. Every "progress" guess the classifier makes carries the same evidence, so the seats went to the three lowest addresses, and a coin count at a high address never got one however often it went up. A guess whose bytes changed during a go the game listened to now gets "played" evidence (0.5) once, which outranks a look-alike guess that never moved while anyone played. The AI still does not know the word "coins": the person picks the row whose value matches the count on screen and names it. Generic: nothing names a game or an address.
- **Scope:** `services/discovery.py`: `begin_go` snapshots every byte's change count; `credit_played` supports each unconfirmed, unrejected guess whose bytes changed since, once, and announces the change so the rewards refresh. `training/service.py`: `begin_go` after the runner begins; `credit_played` after a go unless it showed the game was not listening. **Out of scope:** a new "something you collect" role (the AI cannot tell coins from a level counter yet); showing each reward's value now in the Rewards panel.
- **Acceptance criteria:** with three dead "progress" guesses at constant bytes (weight 2.5) and a weaker one (2.0) on a moving byte, the weaker one is not offered before a go and is after; dead guesses get no credit; credit is given once; nothing is credited without a go begun; in the training service a go credits a guess on a moving byte and not one on a constant byte; suites green.
- **Expected changes:** `src/retro_ai_lab/services/discovery.py`, `src/retro_ai_lab/training/service.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_discovery.py`, `tests/unit/training/test_service.py`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** `test_a_value_that_moves_while_the_ai_plays_earns_its_seat` (discovery); `test_a_go_credits_the_values_that_moved` (service); full suite.
- **Results:** both new tests pass; full suite 1188 passed, 10 skipped; ruff and mypy clean.
- **Commit:** see PR #131. **PR:** #131.
- **Completion report:** `credit_played` adds `played` evidence to every guess that moved during the go, so a coin count that went up while playing takes a seat from junk that never moved; the person names it in the Discovery panel or the Memory table.

### T-110 – Spike: confirm BizHawk Lua ↔ socket protocol
- **Status:** Done
- **Phase:** 1 (BizHawk)
- **Objective:** Fix the communication route between EmuHawk and the app, the message framing and the command set, record it in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §5, and say exactly what still needs a real emulator.
- **Scope:** Documentation only. Checked the BizHawk Lua reference for the `comm`, `memory`, `joypad`, `emu`, `savestate`, `client` and `gameinfo` functions, and Archipelago's `connector_bizhawk_generic.lua` as a worked example of a Lua-side TCP server with newline-delimited JSON on current EmuHawk. Chose LuaSocket (script is the server, app connects) over the built-in `comm.socketServer*` client (needs `--socket_ip/--socket_port` at launch; framing changed in 2.6.2). Defined the commands (`hello`, `read`, `input`, `step`, `screenshot`, `speed`, `save`, `load`, `mode`, `ping`, `bye`), the bundled `step` so a training step is one round trip, and two run modes (hold: emulator paused, frames advance on request; free-run fallback). Decided the test strategy: the Lua script runs in CI under `lupa` (Lua 5.4 in-process) against stubbed EmuHawk globals, plus a Python `BizHawkSimulator` over a real socket. **Out of scope:** any code (T-111 onward); measuring throughput on a real machine (T-117).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §5 documents route, framing, commands with the Lua API behind each, timing, run modes, throughput expectations and the testing approach.
  2. ADR-005 is Accepted with the limitation named (real-emulator behaviour verified in T-117).
  3. Every assumption that only a real EmuHawk can confirm is listed in the "verify" column so T-117 has a checklist.
- **Expected changes:** `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TASK_TRACKER.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Test plan:** n/a (documentation). The protocol itself is tested from T-111 on.
- **Results:** n/a. Full suite unchanged (819 passing).
- **Commit:** see PR. **PR:** #59.
- **Completion report:** The honest limitation: no EmuHawk runs in this environment, so the spike confirms the route from documentation and a widely used working script, not by measurement. Everything that depends on the real emulator (the domain list format, button names per core, whether `emu.frameadvance()` moves a paused emulator, the screenshot file format and cost, `client.speedmode` range) is listed in §5.2 for T-117. The hold-mode question is the one that matters for the adapter's semantics; the free-run fallback keeps the adapter usable either way. `lupa` is added as a dev dependency in T-111 so the Lua script is exercised in CI.
